Written 2026-10-17, about 11:12 AM EDT as this thread's clock reported, by the gap-filling research and planning thread. Read-only: no code, no PRs, no edits to TODO.md or docs/plan. TODO files it. Inputs: `gen-406-galaxy-research-2026-10-17.md` and `docs/design/galaxy/galaxy-laws-research.md` (the 15 requests, GEN.531), `tunable-gaps-2026-10-17.md` (extended here, not redone), section 6 of the atmosphere research master, the body composition master, and sections 4a and 4b of `docs/design/composition/composition-model.md`. Boss's first batch of Gemini results (pasted in this thread at 10:52 AM EDT) is checked in section 7 and labelled "Gemini, unverified" wherever it appears.

Scripts, data and logs behind every number marked COMPUTED or DATA: `/mnt/project-files/reports/gap-filling-plan-2026-10-17-files/`.

## 0. Summary

1. **Of the open gaps, most can be filled by reasoning from verified inputs, and several of Boss's 15 research requests do not need him or Gemini at all.** I list 57 gaps (section 2). By their best fill (section 5): 15 get a derivation (D) from verified relations, 28 a bracket from verified neighbours (B), 11 a theory prior (T), 3 stay labelled placeholders (P). Seven items needed a person or a data set no reasoning can replace; Boss decided all seven (section 8).
2. **Eight fills were tested in this thread, not just proposed** (section 4); the four that matter most: (a) the water share of solids beyond the snow line follows from the oxygen budget of Asplund 2021 abundances and gives 0.17 to 0.32 for a solar mix (0.40 at C/O 0.35), which reproduces the 0.14 to 0.39 bracket of the research draft and puts the 0.5 convention at the all-methane limit; across 1592 real stars (Brewer 2016) it ranges 0.21 to 0.51 (16 to 84 percent, all carbon as CO), so it is a per-star quantity and the "switch" can be replaced by a derived function (BC1); (b) an accreted-halo integral built from Moster 2013 and Giocoli 2008 lands inside the measured stellar-halo range at 5e10 with no tuning (0.071 against 0.056), but it over-accretes by 2 to 3 times over the full range, exceeds 1 above 1e11, and its mass slope disagrees with the D'Souza stack (1.4 to 2.2 against 0.79), which is the first test to run (GR1); (c) the age-velocity normalisation (R4) is answered for the Milky Way by Sharma and Hayden 2020 Table 2 (21.1 km/s at 10 Gyr, exponent 0.441) and scales to other galaxies through the verified Martinsson relation with a 20 percent closure check (GR4); (d) the Comerón 2021 hot-component mass gives a hard upper bound on the thick-to-thin mass ratio that settles the Yoachim against Comerón 2018 conflict (GR3).
3. **Reachability changed.** By 10:59 AM EDT VizieR returned table data, ExoMol and HITRAN front pages answered, and the arXiv search interface returned ids for papers the GEN.406 thread "could not find by title" (Muñoz 2018 III is arXiv:1806.06891, Cooper and Cole 2010 is arXiv:0910.3211, Jordan 2005 is astro-ph/0508219, Pfeffer 2018 is arXiv:1712.00019). So requests R9a (read, answered below), R10, R13 and the Brewer and Fischer tables are reading jobs for us, not research for Boss (section 3 classifies each request A, B or C).
4. **Gemini's first batch is mixed.** I checked 20 claims: 8 agree with verified sources, 7 are contradicted by a verified figure (the occupation mass, the occupation edges, the thick-disc formula, three age-velocity numbers, the nuclear cluster slope), and 5 are unsupported, misattributed or internally inconsistent. I count about 110 distinct claims, about 45 checkable, so a separate checking thread is worth starting (section 7). It must enter through the intake rules of section 7, never as defaults.
5. **One convention makes every fill replaceable:** a stand-in registry with a fill id, a kind (D, B, T, P), an evidence label, assumptions, knobs, a validity range, a falsifier test and the research item that replaces it (section 6). It extends the `stand-in` and `open` markers already Decided for the GEN.472 loader and the target table of GEN.503, so no redesign is needed when Gemini or a paper delivers a value.
6. **What blocks what.** The galaxy gaps feed the 8.1 phase 5 "Galaxy laws build" items (GEN.401, 402, 403, 405, 369, 386); two of them also feed GEN.301's own prerequisites (GR4 through GEN.447, GR9c through GEN.280). The body and atmosphere gaps belong to the composition headline (9.0) and the generation changes of 8.4, apart from the building blocks already back-scattered to 8.1 (section 9).

## 1. Labels used in this plan

**Fill kind** (what the replacement value is):

| Kind | Meaning | Ranked how |
|---|---|---|
| D | derived: a physical or scaling relation computed from verified inputs; the inputs are named | first choice |
| B | bracket: bounded by neighbouring verified quantities; the draw spans the bracket | second |
| T | theory prior: a published or textbook theory supplies the form; the numbers are not measured | third |
| P | placeholder: my own choice or a recalled figure, labelled so, with a bracket | last |

**Evidence label** (how sure each input is), unchanged from the research lanes: READ, DATA, COMPUTED, 2ND-HAND, RECALLED, SNIPPET, NOT REACHED. New for this plan: **LEAD** (a Gemini claim or any claim whose paper has not been opened by us; it can steer where to look and widen a bracket, nothing else) and **POINTER** (a paper id found by search and not yet read).

**Standing rule applied everywhere** (Boss, 3:02 AM EDT): verified and partly verified material is usable unless contradicted; a verified source wins; speculation never beats a verified figure; every theory-based value says so.

**General rules, Decided.** Boss's general rules (typed in the project chat at 12:39 PM EDT on 2026-10-17, relayed by the coordinator): when in doubt, verified wins; when two verified sources are in doubt, the newest wins; when two sources are both in question, the one that takes the most permission wins (his words are 'most permission take wins'; Boss confirmed by card at 1:12 PM EDT on 2026-10-17, relayed in words by the coordinator, that it means the widest range of the two sources). Boss answered a card at 12:40 PM EDT (relayed in words): his six earlier 'widen and keep both' answers stand (arm pitch against bulge fraction, nuclear cluster mass slope, blue to red cluster radius ratio, thick-to-thin height ratio, thick-to-thin mass ratio against Vc, cluster system radius), and the newest-wins rule applies to every conflict that comes after them. **While the network is closed (Boss, 1:17 PM EDT on 2026-10-21, relayed by the coordinator):** arxiv.org, doi.org and crossref are denied until Boss opens Network access, and he said to assume information already included is valid. A lane uses the sources already in the repository or in files he provided as valid and does not block on network verification; a value not grounded in an included source stays a placeholder (labelled recalled, derived, bracket, theory or placeholder) in the registry.

**Ids.** GR1 to GR15 are R1 to R15 of GEN.531 (galaxy; "GR" avoids a clash with the atmosphere research items AR1 to AR15 = GEN.480 to GEN.494 and the body items R1 to R5 = GEN.473 to GEN.476). GX1 to GX5 are galaxy gaps the research found that are not among the 15. BC1 to BC19 are body composition gaps. AT.x are atmosphere rows by their tunable-gaps id (for example AT.a1); AD1 to AD8 are atmosphere data and input gaps from section 6 of the atmosphere master. Mass and time conventions: 200c masses with h = 0.671 inside the generator; the papers use other definitions, so each fill that crosses a definition says so.

## 4. Fills tested in this thread

Each subsection says what was computed, from which inputs, and what would show it wrong. Nothing here is a measurement of a galaxy or a planet. Every input marked READ was opened in this thread; others carry the label they had in the research lanes.

### 4.1 Water share of solids beyond the snow line from the oxygen budget (BC1)

**The derivation (D).** Take the stored star's abundances. Oxygen goes first into the rock: MgO, SiO2, Al2O3, CaO, Na2O, and FeO for the oxidised fraction of the iron; the rest of the iron is metal or FeS (sulfur taken from the same pool); nickel is metal. Carbon is split into refractory organics (counted as solid carbon only, hydrogen ignored, so the organic mass is a lower bound), gas-phase CO (one oxygen per carbon), CH4 (no oxygen) and CO2 (two). Whatever oxygen is left is water. The water share is that water mass over water plus rock plus metal plus refractory carbon. This is bookkeeping, not disk chemistry, and it has no kinetics, hydrated silicates, nitrogen ices or hydrogen in the organics.

**Inputs.** Asplund, Amarsi and Grevesse 2021 (arXiv:2105.01661) Table 2, photospheric log eps: C 8.46, O 8.69, Na 6.22, Mg 7.55, Al 6.43, Si 7.51, S 7.12, Ca 6.30, Fe 7.46, Ni 6.20 (READ).

| Case (Asplund 2021 mix, C/O 0.589, Mg/Si 1.10) | Water share of solids |
|---|---|
| Fe metal and FeS, all carbon as CO gas | 0.227 |
| Fe half FeO, all carbon as CO | 0.193 |
| Fe all FeO, all carbon as CO | 0.168 |
| Fe metal, 30 percent of carbon refractory, rest CO | 0.323 |
| Fe metal, all carbon as CH4 | 0.543 |
| all carbon as CO2 | 0 (more oxygen demanded than exists; not a physical option) |

The research draft's bracket of 0.14 to 0.39 (Marboeuf-derived) is reproduced by CO-dominated carbon with iron metal to oxidised and 0 to about 30 percent refractory carbon (0.17 to 0.32; 0.40 at C/O 0.35). The "about 0.5" convention is the all-methane limit (0.543), which a solar C/O does not reach in a hot inner disc but can in a cold, high-pressure, reducing one. So the two values of the Decided "keep the switch" are the two ends of one derived function of the star and the carbon partition.

**Dependence on the star** (same bookkeeping, one variable changed): C/O 0.35 gives 0.396, 0.589 gives 0.227, 0.80 gives 0.000 (all CO; no oxygen is left for water, the carbon-rich regime); Mg 20 percent low or high moves it to 0.249 or 0.205.

**Across real stars (DATA).** The 1592 stars of Brewer et al. 2016 with C, O, Na, Mg, Al, Si, Ca, Fe, Ni (VizieR J/ApJS/225/32, downloaded 10:59 AM EDT; elements scaled by their own [X/H], sulfur following iron, Asplund solar zero points): all-CO case median 0.341, 16 to 84 percent 0.213 to 0.514, 2.6 percent of stars below 0.05; 30-percent-refractory case median 0.394, 16 to 84 percent 0.305 to 0.528, 0.8 percent below 0.05. So both switch values sit inside the spread among ordinary stars; a fixed share is wrong for any one of them by tens of percent.

**Tunable draw.** The carbon partition (refractory share, CO, CH4) as a Dirichlet with concentration chosen so the 16 to 84 percent range reproduces 0.14 to 0.39 for a solar star; the iron oxidation fraction uniform on 0 to 1 until the redox rows (AT.a1) tie it to the mantle. These two numbers are the same oxidation variables the redox rows need, so BC1, BC8 and AT.a1 should share them (section 9, builder X1).

**Wrong if.** The derived range misses 0.14 to 0.39 for a solar mix (it does not); the Lodders 2003 condensation sequence, once obtained, puts water below 150 K at a different mass fraction by more than 0.1; measured rock-to-ice ratios of outer-disc bodies (comet nuclei, small KBOs, rest of BC7) fall outside the star-by-star spread.

### 4.2 Accreted stellar halo from the subhalo mass function (GR1, GR10, GX5)

**The derivation (D).** The stars of a galaxy's accreted component come from satellites that merged. Let mu = m_v/M0 be a satellite's virial mass at accretion over the host's present mass. Giocoli, Tormen and van den Bosch 2008 (arXiv:0712.1563) Eq. 2 give the universal "unevolved" subhalo mass function: dN/dln(mu) = N0 x^-a exp(-6.283 x^3) with x = mu/a, a = 0.8, N0 = 0.21, independent of host mass (READ, section 3 and Fig. 2). The satellite's stars come from the stellar-to-halo relation: Moster, Naab and White 2013 at z = 0 (READ in the GEN.368 paper check; the parameters are in `intergalactic-and-halo-contents.md` row 2), used at the accretion mass (a stand-in for the relation at the accretion epoch). A satellite below the reionization gate is dark: the Benitez-Llambay and Frenk occupation erf (centre 10^9.4 Msun/h, width 0.25 dex placeholder). The accreted stellar mass is the sum over mu below a ceiling mu_max (Rodriguez-Gomez 2016, abstract: ex situ stars are laid down at larger radii in order of decreasing merger mass ratio, so the halo takes the small mergers).

**Check on my implementation.** The host halo masses it returns (1.65e11, 3.15e11, 8.27e11, 2.00e12, 1.07e13 Msun for M* = 3e9, 1e10, 3e10, 5e10, 1e11) reproduce the worked table of row 2 of the GEN.368 laws table to its printed digits.

**Result (COMPUTED, `acc.py`).** Accreted stars over the host's stellar mass:

| Host M* | mu_max 0.1 | mu_max 0.3 | mu_max 1.0 | GEN.406 placeholder f = 0.05 (M*/5e10)^0.79 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3e9 | 0.003 | 0.017 | 0.030 | 0.0054 |
| 1e10 | 0.005 | 0.024 | 0.041 | 0.014 |
| 3e10 | 0.016 | 0.071 | 0.106 | 0.033 |
| 5e10 | 0.071 | 0.230 | 0.289 | 0.050 |
| 1e11 | 0.71 | 1.05 | 1.12 | 0.087 |
| 2e11 | 2.57 | 2.93 | 3.00 | 0.15 |

Reading it: (1) at 5e10 with mu_max = 0.1 the integral gives 0.071 against the measured 0.056 (range 0.020 to 0.142, six discs of 3.8e10 to 8e10, Harmsen 2017, READ in the GEN.406 note), with no tuning; (2) the all-mu total at 5e10 (0.29) is about three times the Illustris ex situ fraction for Milky Way-sized galaxies (about 0.10, 1-sigma range 0.05 to 0.30; Rodriguez-Gomez 2016 abstract, READ), so the integral over-accretes by a factor of about 2 to 3 even before the halo ceiling; (3) above 1e11 it exceeds 1, which is impossible; the Moster z = 0 relation applied at accretion is wrong there, so the derivation is valid for M* up to about 5e10 and GR14 takes over above; (4) below 3e10 the derived halo is two to three times lighter than the placeholder; (5) the log slope of the derived fraction over 1e10 to 1e11 is 1.4 to 2.2 against 0.79 from the D'Souza stack (which measures an outer light fraction of disc-dominated galaxies, not the same quantity). Items 2 and 5 are the real risk: the derived mass dependence is too steep or the ceiling is wrong.

**Proposed use.** Median by the placeholder power law anchored to the measured six (B); the integral as the shape source below 3e10 and the consistency test above (D); the draw width 0.33 dex (Harmsen scatter, D) widened by half the gap between the two medians below 3e10. The same integral, with each progenitor's infall time drawn from the host's assembly history, gives the halo's age (GR10) and the progenitor-mass-weighted metallicity rule of D'Souza and Bell 2018 (GX5): [Fe/H]_halo = relation(M*_progenitor) - 0.15 (READ in the GEN.406 note).

**Wrong if.** The six Harmsen fractions fall outside 2 sigma of the median at their masses; the integral's slope cannot be brought within 0.3 of 0.79 by a physically motivated ceiling or deposit fraction; Dragonfly's mean 0.009 with 3 of 8 undetected (Merritt 2016, partly) sits outside the 16 to 84 percent range at 1e10 to 1e11.

### 4.3 Age-velocity normalisation for any disc (GR4)

**Milky Way anchor (READ).** Sharma and Hayden 2020 (arXiv:2004.06556, GALAH with Gaia DR2, LAMOST, APOGEE, Kepler and K2) Table 2: vertical dispersion sigma_0,z = 21.1 ± 0.2 km/s for midplane stars of solar metallicity, solar angular momentum and age 10 Gyr, exponent beta_z = 0.441 ± 0.007; radial sigma_0,R = 39.4 ± 0.3, beta_R = 0.251 ± 0.006. Dispersions rise linearly with height |z|; low-alpha and high-alpha stars follow the same relations; valid for 3 to 20 kpc (abstract). So sigma_z/sigma_R = 0.54 at 10 Gyr, inside the 0.5 to 0.6 of Aumer, Binney and Schönrich 2016 (READ in the GEN.406 note).

**Rule for other galaxies (D).** Do not copy the Milky Way values. Use the verified Martinsson et al. 2013 relation, sigma_z,0 = (0.248 ± 0.038) V(2.2 h_R), falling as exp(-R / 2 h_R) (READ), as the age-averaged dispersion at radius R, and the verified exponent beta_z for the shape in age. For a thin disc whose star formation was flat over 0 to tau Gyr, the age average of (t/tau)^beta is 1/(1+beta), so sigma_z(t, R) = sigma_bar(R) (1+beta) (t/tau)^beta. The Sharma and Hayden normalisation then becomes the independent check instead of an input.

**Closure check (COMPUTED from READ values; Milky Way numbers are check targets only).** For V = 220 km/s, h_R = 2.6 kpc, R = 8 kpc: Martinsson gives sigma_bar = 0.248 x 220 x exp(-8/5.2) = 11.7 km/s, so sigma_z(10 Gyr) = 16.9 km/s. Sharma and Hayden give 21.1. The rule is 20 percent low, inside the 15 percent scatter of the ratio plus the 0.441 against 0.5 exponent choice.

**Tunable draw.** beta_z drawn from N(0.441, 0.03); the Martinsson ratio drawn with its 15 percent scatter; sigma_R from beta_R = 0.251 and the ratio sigma_z/sigma_R = 0.54 ± 0.05 (21.1 over 39.4; the label is z over R, so sigma_R = sigma_z / 0.54). Decided (Boss, contradiction sweep card, 7:29 AM EDT 2026-10-21): the ratio is sigma_z/sigma_R = 0.54, which matches 21.1 over 39.4 and `galactic-potential.md`.

**Wrong if.** The Milky Way closure misses by more than 35 percent after the flat-beyond-1.5-h_R correction (Martinsson section 8.3) is applied; a second galaxy with stellar ages disagrees by more than the scatter (none is known; this is what class C means for other galaxies).

### 4.4 Thick-to-thin mass ratio bounded by the hot component (GR3)

Comerón et al. 2021 (arXiv:2101.04478) Eq. 1 gives the mass of the hot component (thick disc plus classical bulge) as log M_h = 0.489 + 0.9161 log M* (READ in the GEN.406 note), so M_h/M* = 0.49, 0.39, 0.35 at M* = 3e9, 5e10, 2e11. With the thin disc taken as M* - M_h at most and the thick disc as M_h minus the classical bulge B (as a share of M*):

M_thick / M_thin = (M_h/M* - B/T_classical) / (1 - M_h/M*), bounded above by M_h / (M* - M_h).

| M* | Upper bound (no classical bulge) | with B/T 0.1 | with B/T 0.3 | Yoachim and Dalcanton formula | Gemini formula (LEAD) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3e9 (Vc about 89) | 0.96 | 0.76 | 0.37 | 0.69 | 2.54 |
| 5e10 (Vc about 181) | 0.64 | 0.48 | 0.15 | 0.14 | 1.17 |
| 2e11 (Vc about 255) | 0.54 | 0.38 | 0.08 | 0.06 | 0.80 |

Both verified sources are consistent with the bound: Yoachim's low values need a classical bulge share of about 0.3 (early-type spirals, B/T 0.35 to 0.38 in row 6 of the laws table) and Comerón 2018's 0.2 to 1 above 120 km/s needs B/T up to about 0.2. The conflict is therefore morphology: the same bound with the drawn B/T of the galaxy gives both. The Gemini formula exceeds the upper bound at all three masses.

**Tunable draw.** B/T_classical from the existing row 6 law; ratio scatter 0.2 dex until a fit with scatter is read. Length ratio 1.15 (Vc/100)^-0.2 and height ratio 2.5 with 30 percent (Yoachim and Dalcanton, READ).

**Wrong if.** The drawn ratios violate the bound in any seed (this is a test, not a tolerance); for galaxies with a known B/T the ratio falls outside Comerón 2018's range.

### 4.5 Reionization and the occupation transition (GR12, GR15)

Benitez-Llambay and Frenk 2020 (READ in the GEN.406 note): half occupation at 10^9.4 Msun/h for early reionization (z_re above 10), a factor of 4 lower for z_re = 4, no galaxy below 3e8 and every halo hosting one above 5e9 (Msun/h); insensitive for z_re above 10 and below 2. The shift is 0.6 dex over 6 units of z_re, 0.1 dex per unit. The global mean is z_re = 7.68 ± 0.79 (Planck 2018 Eq. 18, TT,TE,EE+lowE, mid-point of a tanh model, and z_re below 9.0 at 95 percent; READ, arXiv:1807.06209). That puts the half-occupation mass at about 10^9.17 Msun/h (2.2e9 Msun with h = 0.671) for the mean, and a patchy spread of one unit in z_re moves it by 0.1 dex, which adds in quadrature to the transition width 0.25 dex as sqrt(0.25^2 + 0.1^2) = 0.27. The width itself follows from the two verified edges: from 3e8 to 5e9 is 1.22 dex, and if the edges are 2.4 to 3 sigma from the centre the width is 0.20 to 0.25 dex. So R12 is second order and R15's width is a derived bracket; the centre is the real unknown, and the centre depends on z_re, which the Planck mean fixes.

### 4.6 Cluster half-light radius (GR9a is answered)

Jordan et al. 2005 (astro-ph/0508219, abstract READ): half-light radii measured for thousands of clusters in 100 early-type Virgo galaxies; the average increases with galactocentric distance or with decreasing galaxy surface brightness, decreases with the host galaxy's colour, shows no variation with the cluster's luminosity, and red clusters are about 17 percent smaller than blue; the mean is 2.7 ± 0.35 pc for clusters of colour (g-z) = 1.2 in a host of colour 1.5 at surface brightness mu_z = 21. The Harris file (157 clusters, median 2.9 pc, GEN.406) agrees as a check. Fill: log-normal about 2.7 pc with the three dependences as small multipliers (coefficients to be extracted from the paper by the cluster builder), the red/blue offset 0.83, and no dependence on cluster mass.

### 4.7 Star abundances: Mg, Si, O, C against [Fe/H] (BC2 is answered)

From the 1592 stars of Brewer et al. 2016 with all four elements (DATA, VizieR J/ApJS/225/32, script `brewer_fit.py`; [X/Fe] = a + b [Fe/H], residual sd, zero points on the survey's own scale, number ratios with Asplund 2021 solar values):

| Relation | Slope b | Intercept a | Residual sd (dex) |
|---|---|---|---|
| [C/Fe] | -0.275 | -0.054 | 0.111 |
| [O/Fe] | -0.446 | 0.057 | 0.127 |
| [Mg/Fe] | -0.247 | -0.024 | 0.060 |
| [Si/Fe] | -0.196 | -0.027 | 0.087 |

Residual correlations: C-O 0.52, C-Mg 0.61, C-Si 0.66, O-Mg 0.32, O-Si 0.47, Mg-Si 0.69. Number ratios: C/O median 0.49, 16 to 84 percent 0.37 to 0.58; Mg/Si median 1.07, 16 to 84 percent 0.98 to 1.29. Caution: with no quality cut 0.9 percent of the catalogue lies above C/O 0.8 (maximum 1.00), whereas the paper's cut sample (N = 849 in the tunable-gaps note) has none above 0.66; the cut (Teff and line quality) must be applied before use, which the builder takes from the paper. Validity: [Fe/H] -1.2 to +0.6, F, G and K stars; outside it the draw is a flagged extrapolation, and thick-disc and halo stars need another sample (Hypatia, licence open).

**Fill (V, DATA).** Draw [Fe/H] from the stored star, then a four-vector of residuals from a multivariate normal with these sds and correlations. This replaces the need to store Mg, Si, O and C and keeps them reproducible from the seed. It also gives BC1 and BC4 their inputs.

### 4.8 Mantle redox from an iron-wuestite identity (AT.a1, a2)

For equilibrium between metal and silicate, dIW = 2 log10(a_FeO / a_Fe); in the ideal-solution limit a is the mole fraction. With FeO at 5 to 15 mole percent of the silicate and iron at 85 to 95 percent of the metal, dIW runs from -2.5 to -1.6; real melts add an activity-coefficient correction of a few tenths of a dex. The white-dwarf ensemble is -1.25 ± 0.10 (22 values, COMPUTED and re-run in the tunable-gaps note), so the identity gets the sign and the order (a more iron-oxidised mantle sits higher) and the ensemble sets the zero point within the correction. This is the derivation the "surface from ocean base pressure" row (AT.a1) can use in place of the 1.25 dex-per-dex slope interpolated between a floor and Earth. The iron oxidation fraction is the same variable as in section 4.1.

### 4.9 VizieR search for GR5 and GR3 (2026-10-17)

Boss's decision 6 (section 8) ran the quick VizieR search first. Full report: [vizier-courteau-comeron.md](vizier-courteau-comeron.md). Short:

- GR5: the Courteau data table is not on VizieR and the authors' file is refused, so the residual scatter about Eq. 5 cannot be computed. The raw scatter of log10(Vc/sigma0) of the spirals in two source samples is 0.114 dex (Prugniel, Maubon and Simien 2001, J/A+A/366/68, 69 spirals), 0.119 dex (Pizzella et al. 2005 high-surface-brightness, 40) and 0.123 dex (113 de-duplicated), and 0.104 dex about Hubble-type means. That is inside or within 0.003 dex of the top of the plan's 0.06 to 0.12 bracket, so the plan's replacement rule is not triggered and the fill stays; the true scatter about Eq. 5 is smaller. The paper is ApJ 655, L21 (not ApJ 671, 203).
- GR3 rank 2: Comeron, Salo and Knapen 2018 (A&A 610, A5, VizieR J/A+A/610/A5, 124 galaxies with at least two discs) gives log10(M_thick/M_thin) = 2.113 - 1.086 log10(Vc) with scatter 0.27 dex (0.32 with the single-disc galaxies; the plan had 0.2), a median thick-to-thin height ratio of 4.2 (the plan had 2.5) and a median length ratio of 1.46 (the plan's Yoachim and Dalcanton ratio falls with Vc, this one rises). At Vc 89, 181 and 255 km/s the fit gives 0.99, 0.46 and 0.32 against the plan's hot-component bound 0.98, 0.64 and 0.53, so it lies near the bound and well above the Yoachim and Dalcanton formula at high mass. Taken galaxy by galaxy, 48 of 124 (39 percent) exceed the bound, so the bound test applies to the mean relation or carries a margin.
- References: A&A 562, A121 is the ARRAKIS ring atlas; the 2014 thick-disc paper is A&A 571, A58 (arXiv:1409.0466). The Comeron 2011 and 2012 tables are not on VizieR and are superseded by 2018.

### 4.11 The open-gaps report and Boss's rule of 1:12 PM EDT (2026-10-17)

Boss asked at 12:37 PM EDT for a list of every outstanding item with the closest law for each. The research thread wrote [open-gaps-closest-laws-2026-10-17.md](open-gaps-closest-laws-2026-10-17.md) (64 open lines by area, twelve laws that cover most rows, the free tools that answered). Boss confirmed at 1:12 PM EDT, by card, that "most permission take wins" means the widest range of the two sources: that is Decided and the registry rule in 6.4 now says so.

What changes in this plan:

- **Halo scatter (GR1).** Harmsen 0.33 dex (six discs, read) and the Dragonfly 1.01 dex (eight galaxies, second-hand through the Knapen book) are both in question where the Dragonfly paper has not been read, so the widest applies: the draw width is 1.01 dex, not 0.33. Rule V would give 0.33; the Harmsen scatter stays in the record and stays the test value if the Dragonfly paper is read and found to disagree.
- **Conflicts the report marked "not decided"**, now settled by Boss's rules (two verified sources: the newest wins; the older stays in the record). These are not among the six "widen, keep both" answers. Thick-to-thin length ratio against Vc: Comeron 2018 over Yoachim and Dalcanton 2006. Giant heavy-element mass: Chachan 2025 over Thorngren 2016 (the union stays the bracket where either is in doubt). Moon ice fraction: Cilibrasi 2018 over Sasaki 2010. Planet-star Fe/Mg slope: Plotnykov, Valencia and Ross 2026 (preprint arXiv:2511.17717) over Plotnykov 2020. Both sources in question (M, the widest): the Rogers 2025 and Chakrabarty 2026 water-rich shares stay 0 to 0.87 until read; the Kuiper Belt rock fraction keeps the draw because the dates were not recorded. The report lists these rows as undecided: the building lanes apply the outcome here, and Boss can overrule any of them by card.
- **AD1 methane is filled from free data (partly verified); ammonia and water are still open** (1:55 PM EDT, [ad1-methane-sources-check-2026-10-17.md](ad1-methane-sources-check-2026-10-17.md), after Boss's note of 1:38 PM EDT; the note was AI-written and was checked, not trusted). The HITEMP methane list stops at 746 nm and the paid Karkoschka papers hold the visible bands, but two free routes were found and read, consistent with each other: the Oxford k-tables of Patrick Irwin (14 May 2010) built from Karkoschka and Tomasko 2010 (3,689 wavelengths, 400 nm to 5.0 micron, absorption at 100, 198 and 296 K; the Voigt-Goody temperature formula is printed in Collins 2018), and Karkoschka 1994 and 1998 as NASA PDS tables, volume gbat_0001 (300 to 1050 nm, one cold effective spectrum; the volume is gbat_0001, not go_0016). Where they differ (890 nm band up to 20 percent, weak bands below 700 nm up to about 0.15 dex) both are verified, the 2010 paper is newer so its tables win, and the spread is the draw width. Partly verified only: the Oxford page says its tables are "not yet fully verified" and states no licence, and it is a personal academic page. Still open: ammonia in the visible (only four equivalent widths, 0.05, 0.32, 0.62 and 0.81 nm for the 552, 645, 790 and 930 nm bands, read from a figure), water in the visible (nothing free; the 930 nm water band of 1994 was withdrawn in 1998), methane from 5.0 to 5.5 micron (the Oxford tables stop at 5.0), and the 1e-5 floor below 450 nm (the true weak absorption is not resolved). The ammonia and water stand-in (neighbouring bands, 10 percent) stays as a labelled stand-in in the registry until a measurement is found; it is not a request to Boss (Boss, 3:07 PM EDT 2026-10-18: he has already provided all the AD1 data he can). Fitting a planet's own albedo is still forbidden by NO FIXED CONTENT: the PDS albedo columns are Solar System data and are not copied into the generator.
- **AD8 NH3 activity factor is a labelled stand-in (GEN.501, Boss, 11:53 PM EDT 2026-10-23: "I don't think we can fill this gap, so do the best you can with what you have").** Fill: 1.078 (the Boss-supplied quiet and active rates 1.80e-4 and 1.94e-4 per s at 1 AU, unchecked against Huebner et al. 1992 or Huebner and Mukherjee 2015, which are paywalled and not reachable); bracket 1.05 to 1.10 from the body lane's 170 to 217 nm argument, with the evidence limit that SORCE cycle amplitudes near 200 nm are up to five times SUSIM's, so the true factor could exceed 1.10 (reports/nh3-evidence-check-2026-10-24.md). The quiet lifetime stays 6.7e3 s (Heays 2017, range 5.4e3 to 7.7e3 s). Second-hand reading added by Sources 1 (2026-10-30, GEN.794): Drozdovskaya et al. 2023 (arXiv:2307.11486, section 3.2) take k_pd = 1.8e-4 per s at 1 AU as the average of the quiet and the active Sun of Huebner et al. 1992 and Huebner and Mukherjee 2015 for the NH2 + H channel, and note it is marginally above Heays 2017's 1.5e-4; Biver et al. 2012 (arXiv:1201.4318, the 10P/Tempel 2 paper) quote the same 1.8e-4 as the quiet-Sun value of Huebner et al. 1992. The two statements disagree on whether 1.8e-4 is the quiet value or the quiet-active average, so the sources cannot fix the activity ratio but they do not contradict the bracket: a quiet 1.80e-4 with the factor 1.078 gives an active 1.94e-4, an average of 1.8e-4 gives quiet 1.73e-4 and active 1.87e-4, and both stay inside the 1.05 to 1.10 bracket; the page that holds the table (phidrates.space.swri.edu) is not reachable from the lane machines. Source state (GEN.794, 2026-10-30): replaced by a computation, rows NA1 and NA2 of section 6.64: the factor is 1.07, the cross section of the Leiden database with the SORCE SOLSTICE, UARS SUSIM and UARS SOLSTICE records (`scripts/nh3_activity_factor.py`), bracket 1.05 to 1.15; the Boss-supplied 1.078 sits inside it. Falsifier: the NH3 line of Huebner and Mukherjee 2015 outside 1.05 to 1.15.
- **How the methane data is fetched at build time** (Boss's offline rule, plan 6.5): the build downloads the 0.24 MB band-parameter extract (wavenumber, wavelength, absorption at 100, 198 and 296 K, line-spacing ratio) from the Oxford page, with the PDS single spectrum as the test value, checks each file against the sha256 recorded in the data folder's README, and falls back to the labelled stand-in if the host does not answer; tests run offline on a few rows of the extract. Nothing is stored in the repository. The data and its source URLs, hashes and terms are in `/mnt/project-files/data/ad1-methane/` for now.
- **Corrections to the pasted note:** the PDS volume is gbat_0001; the Neptune paper is Karkoschka and Tomasko, Icarus 211(1), 780 to 797 (2011), DOI 10.1016/j.icarus.2010.08.013; the 2010 paper's eleventh data set is HST Jupiter images with Ganymede as the light source; PMC5105178 is Sanchez-Lavega 2016 (Nature Communications 7, 13262, Saturn), and the 0.515 bar level belongs to the F336W filter, not F225W; Uranus methane: Karkoschka and Tomasko 2009 gives 1.4 to 3.2 percent, the 4 percent is Voyager Model F or Sromovsky 2011, so the widest bracket is 1.4 to 4 percent. Authors are confirmed: Karkoschka and Tomasko for 2009 and 2010, Karkoschka alone for 1994.
- **Boss's answer, 1:23 PM EDT** (typed in the project chat, relayed by the coordinator): AD1 he will research himself (Karkoschka and Tomasko 2010 and the related papers, B5 and B6 of papers-to-fetch.md); he then sent a source note, and the check (above) filled methane, so what was left of AD1 was ammonia and water in the visible (Boss, 3:07 PM EDT: he has already provided all the AD1 data he can, so that is no longer a request to him). He reads the other lines where math cannot help (GR7, BC6, BC11, BC18, AT.b5, AT.n7, AT.m3, m4 and m11, AT.k7, T15) as having no data to find: each stays a labelled stand-in at the widest bracket under the widest-range rule and is replaced when a measurement exists. GR7 stays widened with both sources kept (his 11:19 AM EDT answer).
- **Licences (Boss, 2:35 PM EDT, typed in the project chat; GEN.500 removed):** every paper and dataset Boss acquired was obtained legally and is usable. The raw papers and downloaded datasets stay out of the public repository and are never redistributed or sold; the research results and the values derived from them may be published, and the program is free forever. No licence audit gates the composition tables (GEN.410, GEN.427, GEN.438); the licence column stays as a record and takes `open` where none is found.
- **Free routes found.** BC13 weights and limits are in the free IAEA GSR Part 3 (read; only the neutron weighting function is missing). SO2 and H2S Rayleigh cross sections can be computed from polarizability and anisotropy (the King factor), so the Bogaard 1978 dependency is dropped and the paper becomes a check. Lodders 2003 is behind a captcha: nothing is blocked, the derived water share reproduces 0.14 to 0.39.
- **Citation fixes** (each checked in Crossref or arXiv): "Renzini 1997" (astro-ph/9711072) is Buzzoni 1997; "Yu and Ho 2018" for the pitch-angle result is Yu and Ho 2019 (ApJ 871, 194); Hart 2017 is MNRAS 472, 2263 (arXiv:1708.04628, arm shares and pitch angles; Core 1 checked it on arXiv and Crossref), not MNRAS 468, 1850, and it holds no bulge result; the fO2 buffer must not be cited to O'Neill and Eggins 2002 (activity coefficients) but to Hirschmann 2021 for the iron-wuestite calibration; Badhwar and O'Neill is two papers (1994 and 1996), the notes must name the one the code follows; the Plotnykov slope 0.94 is the 2025 preprint. The galaxy and stellar documents are corrected in the same change; the fO2 and Badhwar citations are not in a project file yet, so the items that will write them carry the correction.

### 4.12 The citations pass (2026-10-24, GEN.792)

The Citations lane looked for the values of every source Boss cannot read in the open works that reproduce them, and audited every file Boss uploaded; the report is reports/secondhand-citations-2026-10-24.md in the project files (Parts 1 to 10, kept in the project files). The rows it touched carry a "Citations pass" clause in their Fill cell above. Sources Boss cannot access, never to be listed for him again: Fray and Schmitt 2009, ICRP 103 full report, Karkoschka 1994, Karkoschka and Tomasko 2009/2010, Tomasko et al. 2008, Ayers 1980, Frost 1991, Hirschmann 2012, Palme and O'Neill 2014, JPL Publication 19-5, Bogaard 1978, Huebner 1992 and Huebner and Mukherjee 2015.

**The two source flags (GEN.793).** (1) CFC-12 forcing coefficient: Myhre, Highwood, Shine and Stordal 1998 (GRL 25, 2715; Boss's upload, read as a scan, Table 3 on page 2718) gives the best-estimate constant for CFC-12 as 0.33 W m^-2 per ppbv, against 0.28 in IPCC 1990 and 0.25 for CFC-11 (IPCC 0.22). The plan's 0.33 is right and stays. The 0.32 in IPCC TAR Table 6.2 is the Third Assessment Report's own later revision, not Myhre's value; use 0.33 where the source is cited as Myhre 1998 and 0.32 only where it is cited as IPCC 2001. No code in `src` carries either number. (2) NH3 photolysis activity factor 1.078 (GEN.501): Irwin et al. 2019 (arXiv 1812.05383) is an NH3 absorption line-list and retrieval for giant-planet atmospheres and contains no photolysis rate or solar-activity ratio, so it cannot confirm or change the factor. The open evidence is: quiet Sun 1.8e-4 per s at 1 au (Biver et al. 2012, quoting Huebner et al. 1992); 1.8e-4 as the quiet and active average (Drozdovskaya et al. 2023, quoting Huebner 1992 and Huebner and Mukherjee 2015); 1.54e-4 from the Leiden database scaled to 1 au; no active-Sun NH3 rate in any open work reached (the Crovisier 1994 and Bockelee-Morvan and Crovisier chapter tables were not reachable). The factor therefore stays a labelled stand-in at 1.078 with the bracket 1.05 to 1.10, tier T4, and the quiet rate stays 1.54e-4 to 1.8e-4. It is replaced by the Huebner and Mukherjee 2015 NH3 quiet and active line, or by a computation from the Cheng 2006 cross section with measured solar spectra.

Still open after the pass (labelled stand-ins stay): Bogaard per-wavelength King factors, Fray validity windows (BC12), the Wolff spectra as numbers, the Tomasko 2008 haze spectrum, the dose-against-depth tables of Cooper 2001, Patterson 2012 and Nordheim 2018, Gronoff 2009 and Dartnell, JPL 19-5 revisions outside GEOS-Chem, the visible water absorption, Walker 1981 and Elkins-Tanton 2008, Dotter EEP detail, and brines other than H2SO4-H2O.

## 5. Ranked fill for every gap

Reading the tables: **Kind** is the kind of the first-ranked fill (D, B, T, P, section 1). **Fill** gives the ranked options in order; the first one is used unless its inputs are missing. **Draw** is how the uncertainty enters generation as a tunable draw (distribution and width); a width marked (P) is my own choice, labelled so, and a width marked (V) comes from a verified scatter. **Wrong if** is the test that would show the fill false; each one becomes the falsifier test of the registry (section 6). **Result lands as** says what a Gemini or paper result changes. Where the draw says "none", the quantity is computed, not drawn, and the scatter comes from its inputs.

### 5.4 Count of the best fills

61 lines. By the kind of the first-ranked fill: **D 15** (GR3, GR4, GR9a, GR10, GR12, GR15, GX1, GX5, BC1, BC2, BC15, BC16, AT.a1/a2, AD3, AD6), **B 28**, **T 15**, **P 3** (BC11, AT.b5, AT.n7).

## 6. One convention for every filled value: the stand-in registry

Goal: any value that is not a verified measurement says so in the code, in the docs and in the loader report, and can be replaced by a result without a redesign. This extends what is Decided: the GEN.472 loader already carries source states (verified, partly verified, open, stand-in), Dirichlet where only a range is known, and a table version that bumps the generator epoch; GEN.503 already holds a target table (row id, statistic, value, sigma, source, label, validity range, selection-model id). The registry adds the fill itself and the link between them. Names below are proposals for the builder; nothing here exists in the repository.

### 6.1 The record

One record per fill, in a single module (proposed `planetgen/fills.py`), data not behaviour:

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `fill_id` | the id of this plan (GR4, BC1, AT.a1); permanent, never reused |
| `kind` | D, B, T or P (section 1) |
| `evidence` | READ, DATA, COMPUTED, 2ND-HAND, RECALLED, SNIPPET, LEAD; one label per input, worst one wins for the record |
| `source_state` | the GEN.472 state it maps to: D from READ or DATA inputs is "partly verified" (verified once the closure check passes); B is "partly verified"; T is "stand-in (theory)"; P is "stand-in (placeholder)"; anything resting on a LEAD is "open" |
| `form` | formula or table reference |
| `assumptions` | one line each, as written in this report |
| `validity` | the range inside which the fill holds, and what the code does outside it: draw with a doubled width and count it as an extrapolation |
| `knobs` | distribution, centre, width, with (V) or (P) |
| `target_id` | the GEN.503 target row it is tuned or checked against, if any |
| `falsifier` | the test id that fails when "wrong if" in section 5 is true |
| `replaced_by` | the research item that supersedes it (for example GEN.531 request id, or the reading job) |
| `sources` | citations; each marked READ only if opened by a thread, with the date and the thread |
| `licence` | for data tables, the licence or "public record" |
| `epoch` | the generator epoch in which this version took effect |

### 6.2 In code and in docs

Constants keep the style of `tuning.py`: a docstring with the source and the label. A filled value adds one line: `Fill GR4 (D, COMPUTED): ...` and reads its numbers from the registry rather than repeating them. In documents the tag is `[fill GR4 · D · COMPUTED]` right after the figure, and the figure is a range or a distribution, never a bare number. A sentence that rests on a LEAD says "Gemini, unverified" in the same sentence. The loader's start-up report prints counts by kind and by state, and the number of draws made outside a validity range; a build check fails if a fill with `source_state` "open" feeds a stored value (so unverified material can never reach a seed silently).

### 6.3 Replacing a stand-in

1. The result arrives (a paper read by a thread, a table downloaded, a Gemini claim).
2. The owner classifies it: verified (opened and read), partly verified (second-hand but consistent), or LEAD.
3. A LEAD may only change the registry's `knobs` width (widen a bracket) or add a comparison to the falsifier; the centre and the kind do not change.
4. A verified result replaces the fill: new record version, `replaced_by` filled, old record kept as superseded with its epoch, falsifier test updated to the new target.
5. If the replacement changes any stored value, it is a table version change and therefore a generator epoch bump, riding the pending epoch (Decided); if it only widens a draw's width in a way that changes stored values, same.
6. The docs tag changes from `[fill ...]` to a plain citation.

### 6.3a Ranges of the galaxy shape dials (GEN.609)

Every slider on the Generate page and every `planetgen plan` shape flag has a range, a source for it and one of the labels verified, derived, bracket, theory or placeholder, kept in the lever table (`planetgen.galaxy.dials.LEVERS`) and copied here by `python3 -m planetgen.galaxy.dials --write`. A test fails on a row with no source or no label, and on this block going stale. Network access is closed, so a range whose source was not read in the repository or in the files Boss provided stays a bracket or a placeholder, named as such; the slider ships all the same.

| `--richness` | 1 to 3 systems per solar mass | bracket | T3 | L0 | the derived mean system mass runs 0.47 (old stars) to 0.79 solar masses (stars just formed), 1.3 to 2.1 systems per solar mass (the generator's own laws); the band 1 to 3 widens it by about a factor of 1.4 |
| `--arm-count` | 1 to 10 | placeholder | T4 | L0 | Hart et al. 2017 counts 1 to 5 or more arms; the ceiling of 10 is a placeholder |
| `--pitch-angle-deg` | -90 to 90 degrees, at least 1 away from 0 | placeholder | T4 | L0 | Hart 2017 draws 10 to 40; the full quarter turn is a placeholder; under 1 degree from 0 is refused because a flat pitch divides by zero (density-limits report, 10-21) |
| `--arm-contrast` | 0 to 1 | placeholder | T4 | L0 | 0 to 1 because the density cannot go negative (theory); the 0.4 default is the first plan's hand value, no source |
<!-- dial-ranges:end -->

### 6.4 Conflicts

| Case | Rule |
|---|---|
| Verified against verified | for the six cases Boss answered before 12:40 PM EDT, neither wins: widened bracket or mixture, both stay in the record (GX2, GX3, GR7 and the three thick-disc and cluster-radius conflicts of 7.2a); for every conflict found after that, the newest source wins (Boss, 12:39 PM EDT) and the older stays in the record |
| Two sources both in question | the one that takes the most permission wins (Boss, 12:39 PM EDT); Decided at 1:12 PM EDT (his card): 'most permission' means the widest range of the two sources |
| Verified against Gemini or any LEAD | verified wins; the LEAD is logged as contradicted with the verified figure beside it |
| Partly verified against LEAD | partly verified wins unless the LEAD points to a primary source that is then read |
| LEAD with no contradiction | usable only to widen a bracket; stays "open" |
| Theory against data | data wins as soon as it is READ; the theory fill is kept as the label of what the draw was |
| Two theory fills | the simpler one; the other is recorded as an alternative in `assumptions` |
| Derived against measured | a derived fill that disagrees with a READ measurement fails its falsifier and is demoted to P until corrected |
| Paper against a second-hand value in our files | the paper wins; both values stay in the record and the fill is marked "paper read, differs" (section 4.10 lists twelve) |
| A secondary source (a paper that cites a paper) and nothing better | accepted by default (Boss, 8:16 PM EDT 2026-10-29); evidence label 2ND-HAND with the citing work named; a primary that is read later replaces it |
| No source at all | a stand-in is allowed if it is registered with tier and tweak level; hand-tuning without a registry row is not |

### 6.5 Downloaded data and offline tests

Decided (Boss, 2026-10-17, between 11:19 AM and 11:23 AM EDT, relayed by the coordinator): line lists and star catalogues (HITRAN, ExoMol, Brewer 2016, Hypatia) are downloaded at build time. No derived table of them is stored in the repository. The cost is that a build needs the network, so the design must also say how tests run offline: tests never reach the network; they read either fixtures the test builds itself from synthetic values or a cache of earlier downloads kept outside the repository, and the record's `licence` field names the dataset licence. The data work of AD1 to AD8, BC2 and BC19 (group X9 and the BC2 and BC19 parts of X6 and X7) is gated on that design (the download step, the cache location, the fixtures and the offline test rule), not on reading the licences.

Decided (Boss, 12:46 AM EDT on 2026-10-18, card 'Shared step first', as the coordinator relayed): GEN.548 builds the one shared download step first: the standard User-Agent of API.31, a sha256 check, a cache outside the repository in a folder `update.sh` fills, a labelled stand-in when the host is down, and offline fixtures for the tests. Its three datasets follow, and the data jobs AD1 to AD8, BC2 and BC19 reuse the same step. No download step is merged yet: AD1 methane is a design and a source check only, so its extract is built on the shared step once GEN.548 lands.

Standing rule (Boss, 12:47 AM EDT on 2026-10-18, project chat, as the coordinator relayed): Downloads of static files take place at setup (the step `update.sh` runs, into a cache folder outside the repository) unless the data needs to be constantly updated; any code that fetches data at run time must say in its design why the data needs constant updates (Boss, 12:47 AM EDT on 2026-10-18, project chat, as the coordinator relayed). "Build time" in this section and in the items means this setup step.

Decided (Boss, 5:54 PM EDT on 2026-10-21, project chat, as the coordinator relayed; this revises his 'Require the data' answer of 9:38 AM EDT on 2026-10-18 and answers the refuse-or-fall-back question of every AD dataset, GEN.594): every dataset has the best possible fallback if its files are not there, but the generate process must be told explicitly to disregard missing files. A run with a missing file refuses by default and names the file, its URL and the setup command; with the explicit option (GEN.599) it uses the fallback and prints each one in the run header; there is no silent fallback. The updater and the downloader print the URL of every file they load (GEN.600). The README lists every external file with its URL and install step (DOC.72).

As built (GEN.548, the shared step; the datasets themselves follow in the same item). `planetgen/datasets/fetch.py` holds the one step every download job uses: a `Dataset` (key, citation, licence, files, what it replaced and its registry id) lists its files as `Piece`s with an https URL, an exact size and a sha256, and the entries live in `planetgen/datasets/catalogue.py`. `python -m planetgen.cli.datasets fetch` is the setup half: `update.sh` (step 3) and `install.sh` run it once as root. It sends the User-Agent that `planetgen/util/user_agent.py` builds (the one place that spells it, API.31 reuses it), reads at most the stated size, refuses a wrong size or hash and keeps nothing of it, writes by atomic rename into `/var/cache/planetgen/datasets/<key>/` (`PLANETGEN_DATA_DIR` moves it) and never raises: a host that is down gives a `missing` result with a reason per file and exit code 1, which the scripts print as a warning. Files already there with the right hash are not requested again, and a catalogue entry with a new hash replaces the old file. `fetch.locate(dataset)` is the run-time half: it opens no connection, hashes each file once per process, and returns the folder or that the dataset is missing with its label (`missing (AD1, replaces ...)`); a reader that needs the files raises `fetch.DataMissing`, and what a reader does without them is that item's own decision (star composition requires them, GEN.590, below). A test (`test_datasets_fetch.py`) keeps the generator's packages free of any module that opens a URL. Offline tests pass a fake `opener` that serves bytes the test built and whose sha256 the test computes, so no test reaches the network and no fixture of downloaded data is committed.

Each is "stand-in" in the loader report until the files are in the cache folder and "partly verified" after (the files are the sources' own; the readers interpolate them). The follow-up that wires the readers into the stellar-composition modules moves each fill's `replaced_by` from a plan to code.

As built (GEN.590, wiring SC1 to SC3). Boss's answer on the card 'Should star composition read the downloaded data files?' (12:55 PM EDT on 2026-10-18, as the coordinator relayed) was **Require the data**: no fallback value stands in, so a "stand-in" in the table above is now what the fill replaced, and "missing" is what the loader report says until the files are in the cache folder. `physics/stellar_composition.py` reads the files: a main-sequence star gets its MIST luminosity, temperature, radius and lifetime at its mass, age and [Fe/H] (`isochrones`); a white dwarf gets its radius at its temperature and its layers from the Bedard et al. sequences (`white_dwarf_models`; a hydrogen atmosphere takes the thick-layer case, any other the thin one; `star_makeup` now needs the white dwarf's `teff_k`); a neutron star gets the outer crust of one of the 20 nuclear mass models, picked by a draw added at the end of the star's draws (`neutron_star_matter`; no earlier draw moves). The Chandrasekhar table of GEN.439 (`white_dwarf_mass_radius.json` and its build script) and the nickel-to-krypton crust table were removed with the stand-ins they were. A kind that reads a file raises `fetch.DataMissing` without it. **Bulk generation refuses to start** while any catalogue file is missing: `run_galaxy.require_datasets` runs after the math check in every bulk CLI run, in the Sector page's neighbourhood generation and its API route; the Generate page's jobs get a second step, "Check the data" (`python -m planetgen.cli.datasets status`, exit 1), before the reset, so a missing file stops a job before anything is written; `update.sh` and `install.sh` warn at the end. Nothing stored changes (the makeup is derived on read and no generator code calls it yet), so there is no generator epoch. Still stand-ins after this item, and why: the brown-dwarf radius and the hydrogen-burning limit against Z (BHAC15 rows at 1 Gyr, solar Z) stay, because the MIST grid starts at 0.1 solar masses, above every brown dwarf; the inner crust and core of a neutron star need an equation of state integrated (the 21 tables are on disk, nothing integrates them yet; a star's radius already comes from the measured band of `physics/neutron_star.py`, GEN.463); the helium-core and oxygen-neon white dwarfs have no layers (the sequences are carbon-oxygen). Datasets of other items (AD1 to AD8) decide for themselves whether a missing file refuses, falls back or both; every dataset in `catalogue.ALL` is required until one of them adds a field that says otherwise.

As built (GEN.599, Boss 5:53 PM EDT on 2026-10-21): the refusal of GEN.590 stays the default, and a run told in so many words to disregard missing data takes the best fallback instead (`fetch.set_disregard_missing_data`; `--disregard-missing-data`, the Generate page's box, the API's `disregard_missing_data`; every worker takes the run's option from its run record). Each dataset states its `fallback` in the catalogue and a reader asks `Located.use_stand_in`. The fallbacks, each now labelled `STAND_IN` in `stellar_abundances.RELATIONS` and named in the star's `open_items`: SC1 none (a main-sequence star has no luminosity, temperature, radius or lifetime, because the repository holds no track; the Baraffe et al. 2015 rows cover brown dwarfs only), SC2 the zero-temperature Chandrasekhar mass-radius table of GEN.439 (`white_dwarf_mass_radius.json` and `scripts/build_white_dwarf_table.py`, restored from before GEN.590; no layers), SC3 the Chamel and Haensel 2008 Table 2 outer crust (`stellar_abundances.NEUTRON_STAR_OUTER_CRUST`, restored; one sequence and no crust model name). The refusal message names every file to download with its URL and the setup command. The run's own record is its command line in the run history (the flag is in it); no table was added.

| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | what stands in (constant) | replaced_by |

| IR6 | T | T3 | L0 | a CIA band covered by under half of its width in a file is zero, and values beyond the measured temperatures are held at the nearest one; pairs with no file (CH4-CH4, most noble-gas pairs, every pair with water) are zero | further HITRAN pairs; the water pair continuum |
| IR7 | T | T1 on Earth, T6 elsewhere | L0 | the water vapour profile: Manabe and Wetherald's (1967) relative humidity (`HUMIDITY_TOP` 0.02) with the surface humidity the mixture's own, no vapour above the cold trap, at most half the layer (`MAX_WATER_FRACTION`, T7) | a computed humidity (moist convection with a mixing parameterisation) |
| IR8 | P | T7 | L0 | the sunlight a haze takes aloft: a Gaussian of width `SUNLIGHT_WIDTH_LN_P` 1.0 in ln P. The share and the pressure are inputs of the caller (Titan: 60 percent at 30 mbar in the check, McKay et al. 1991); the fixed point supplies them from the aerosol model since GEN.851 (IR10) | none: the caller's inputs |
| IR9 | P | T6 to T7 | L0 | the heat capacity of a gas with no NASA Glenn fit (`radiative_convective._cp_over_r`, GEN.852): the gas table's values at 100, 300 and 1000 K interpolated and held at the ends (T6, so low above 1000 K for a polyatomic gas); a gas with none takes a rigid-rotor count of its atoms, 5/2, 7/2 and 4 + (3N - 6)/2 in units of R (T7). Only the dry adiabat reads it, in proportion to the gas's share of the air | a NASA fit or a JANAF table for each further gas |
| IR10 | T | T6 | L0 | the tropopause is a model level: `LAYERS` 40 on a log-pressure grid up to `TOP_FRACTION` 1e-4 of the surface pressure, so the reported tropopause pressure moves in steps of about 26 percent. Nothing outside the module reads it; the surface temperature of an Earth-like air moves by under 0.1 K when the level steps (test_radiative_convective_real) | a sub-layer tropopause from the lapse rate, or more layers if a reader needs it |
| IR11 | D | T1 | L0 | the fitted greenhouse table (`physics/greenhouse_table.py`, `physics/data/greenhouse_table.json`): a network of the 16 features of an air (GEN.850) fitted to the model's surface temperature on the airs the generator makes, perturbed copies and designed families; its error is the file's own stated validation, the table is refused when the datasets it was fitted to change, and an air outside the fitted range is held at the range's edge | the model itself, if it becomes fast enough to call at generation |
| IR12 | P | T9 | L3 | the cloud's longwave effect: the sky sends out `(1 - CLOUD_LONGWAVE_SHARE) * clear-sky flux`, `CLOUD_LONGWAVE_SHARE` 0.13 at the Earth's surface humidity `EARTH_HUMIDITY` 0.77, in proportion to the surface humidity (to 1.3 times at most), nil without water vapour. CERES EBAF gives 26.5 of 266 W/m2 (0.10), which leaves the Earth twin of the fixed point at 284.6 K, the clear-sky model's own bias; 0.13 puts it at 287.9 K, the measured 288 K | a cloud model (condensate path from the saturation excess, particle size, cloud top) |
| IR13 | P | T7 | L0 | the share of the sunlight a haze takes aloft (`aerosols.haze_absorbed_share`): the tholin absorption at each wavelength (the optics' own law) over the star's blackbody from 200 to 3000 nm, on a slant path of `HAZE_AIR_MASS` 2 (the sphere-averaged secant), capped at `HAZE_SHARE_CAP` 0.95; the level is where half of the share is taken from the top, in the haze's mass profile (`haze_fall`) | a radiative-transfer solution of the haze and the air together, with the haze's scattering |
| IR14 | P | T5 | L0 | the adiabat of an air with no water but some methane (GEN.1030): methane is the condensable, at the mole fraction of the surface (the deep abundance held up the column, no depletion by cloud), saturating on `saturation`'s curve for CH4 from 30 K to `METHANE_TABLE_TOP_K` 188 K, with the latent heat from its slope (Clausius and Clapeyron); the other gases are the background. The adiabat is the same moist pseudo-adiabat as water's, so no new law | the cloud-base mixing ratio and the depletion above it (Lindal et al. 1987 and 1992 for Uranus and Neptune), which a cloud model would give |
| IR15 | D | T1 | L0 | the heat capacity of molecular hydrogen below `EQUILIBRIUM_HYDROGEN_BELOW_K` 200 K (GEN.1030): ortho and para forms in equilibrium, from the rotational levels of `H2_ROTATION_TEMPERATURE_K` 87.55 K (B 60.853 cm-1, Herzberg 1950) and the nuclear spin weights 1 and 3; it joins the NASA fit at 200 K. The equilibrium (a catalysed troposphere) is the assumption: with no conversion the cold heat capacity would be that of normal hydrogen, 2.5 to 2.7 R at 60 to 100 K | the observed lapse rates of Uranus and Neptune in the 0.1 to 1 bar layer, which sit between the two cases (Gierasch 1985) |

Not stand-ins: the 40 layers, the model top at 1e-4 of the surface pressure, the g grid of 10 points, the diffusivity 1.66 (Elsasser), and the numerical tolerances of the solver.

Not stand-ins and so not listed: `LAMBDA_KEPT` (theory: escape speed six times the rms speed, 6^2 x 3/2), `REFERENCE_AGE_GY` (the age the threshold is stated for), `UNLISTED_LIMIT` and the numerical floors.

### 6.8 Stand-ins of the hours outside in a day (GEN.768)

Every number the hours-outside model (`physics/outside_hours.py`, GEN.768) holds that is not a verified measurement has a row here: the reference body, the garments' insulation and thermal power, the work rate, the sweat limit and the shift. Earth's sea-level pressure and temperature (`earth_pressure_kpa`, `earth_temperature_k`) are standard-atmosphere definitions, not stand-ins. Nothing is drawn: the hours follow from the stored equipment levels, the temperatures, the daylight and the dose.

### 6.9 Stand-ins of how a rendered body is drawn (MAP.328)

The slow turn of a rendered body on its axis (`static/spin.js`) takes the body's stored rotation period, axis and tilt (all real, from the generator) and runs the period through one view-time law so a turn can be seen. The law is not physics: real periods run from hours to hundreds of days, a hundred times wider than the 20 s to 600 s a screen can show a turn in without strobing or looking stuck, so no straight proportion fits. The law keeps the order of speeds and is the same for every body.

<!-- rendered-body-standins:begin -->
| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SP1 | P | T9 | L4 | spin.js FASTEST_SECONDS_PER_TURN 20, SLOWEST_SECONDS_PER_TURN 600, FASTEST_PERIOD_HOURS 2, SLOWEST_PERIOD_HOURS 10000 (seconds per turn on screen = 20 x 30^t, t the log position of the period from 2 h to 10000 h, clamped; a tidally locked body is not turned by this law but kept with its face toward what it orbits) | none (a display choice) | every body drawn as a sphere in the system views and on object pages; the galactic views turn nothing | the spin.test.mjs tests of the cap, the floor and the order of speeds | a reader-set speed, if one is ever asked for | design (Boss, 2:48 AM EDT 2026-10-25: rendered objects turn slowly on their axes; the range is fitted so the fastest still shows a turn and the slowest is not stuck); Boss, 4:47 PM EDT 2026-10-30 (UX.244): a 46.8-day rotation looked frozen at the old 1000 h clamp, so the slow end moved to 10000 h, and a locked body keeps its day side toward the star |
<!-- rendered-body-standins:end -->

### 6.10 Stand-ins of the radius valley (GEN.722)

The radius-valley survey (`physics/radius_valley.py`, `docs/design/composition/radius-valley-tuning.md`) tunes the atmosphere recipes' envelope and loss knobs to the valley of the planets Kepler found. Four numbers in it are not verified measurements. Nothing it computes is read by the generator yet, so no generated value depends on these rows.

| RV2 | P | T9 | L3 | radius_valley.CORE_MASS_SIGMA 0.25 (log-normal width of the core mass, in ln units; narrowed from 0.4 with the onset age of AM81 so that both valley numbers come out, GEN.822) | fixed (0.4 puts the period slope at -0.16 and the radius at 10 d at 1.9 with the 1 Gyr onset) | the survey of hosts 0.5 to 1.4 solar masses | a width of 0.4 with the same onset leaves the period slope outside its error | a published width of the core-mass distribution | TUNED (no published width read; swept with the onset age, test_radius_valley::test_the_valley_slope_and_location_follow_the_published_values_across_the_recipe_bracket) |

### 6.12 Stand-ins of the orbit elements (GEN.727)

The eccentricity and inclination laws of planets and moons (`physics/orbit_elements.py`) and what the code does with them. Kinds, tiers and tweak levels as in 6.1 and in the list of every stand-in. The anchor of the first three rows is the abstract of Van Eylen et al. 2019 (AJ 157, 61, arXiv:1807.00549), read for this item; the paper is of small planets close to their stars, and the laws are used for every planet at every distance, which is row OE4.

| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | evidence | constant | knobs | validity | replaced_by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| OE1 | B | T1 | L0 | READ (Van Eylen et al. 2019 abstract: multi-planet systems consistent with a half-Gaussian of 0.083, +0.015 -0.020) | orbit_elements.MULTI_SIGMA | 0.083, fixed | planets of systems with two or more planets | a law fitted to planets of every period and size |
| OE2 | B | T1 | L0 | READ (the same abstract: 0.76, +0.21 -0.12, of single transiting systems have a moderate eccentricity) | orbit_elements.SINGLE_MODERATE_FRACTION | 0.76, fixed | a system with one planet | the same |
| OE3 | B | T1 | L0 | READ (the same abstract: a Rayleigh law that peaks at 0.26, +0.04 -0.06) | orbit_elements.SINGLE_RAYLEIGH_MODE | 0.26, fixed | the moderate share of single planets | the same |
| OE4 | T | T2 | L0 | the laws of OE1 to OE3 used outside the planets they were fitted to (Kepler's, mostly under 100 days) and with the planets the generator counts, not the ones a transit survey finds | orbit_elements.draw_planet_eccentricity | the three above | every planet | radial-velocity and direct-imaging eccentricities by period and mass |
| OE5 | P | T4 | L0 | design (the laws put almost nothing beyond it; the periapsis cut comes first for a close orbit) | orbit_elements.MAX_ECCENTRICITY | 0.9, fixed | every planet and moon | none needed |
| OE6 | B | T2 | L0 | Fabrycky et al. 2014: Rayleigh mutual inclination of 1.0 to 2.2 degrees (read in an earlier session, not again for this item); a planet's own angle to the plane is that over the square root of two | orbit_elements.MUTUAL_INCLINATION_SIGMA_DEG | 1.6 degrees, fixed | planets | a law of the plane scatter of giants and of systems beyond the Kepler range |
| OE7 | P | T3 | L0 | the forced eccentricities of the regular satellites of the giants run from 0.001 to 0.03 and the Moon 0.055; the range is the one the tide of GEN.700 already drew from | orbit_elements.REGULAR_MOON_ECCENTRICITY_RANGE | 0.001 to 0.01, log | moons of disk, impact, ring or binary origin | a forced eccentricity from the resonances and neighbours of the moon system |

| OE14 | P | T4 | L0 | design: a planet or captured moon whose eccentricity at the star's final age is within 1e-4 of the drawn one keeps its orbit; the circularisation time (OE10) is read again from the body's radius as it then is, so GEN.985 changes a body only where the age of the star rose enough to matter (measured: 134 of 327 planets move at all, 53 by more than 0.01 or 5 percent) | planets.ECCENTRICITY_SETTLE_TOLERANCE | 1e-4, fixed | planets and captured moons | none needed |
| OE15 | P | T4 | L0 | design: a rocky planet or moon made when the star's age was within 2 percent of its final age keeps its air, class, spin and dose (GEN.992); above that the decay clock and the heat history it read are off by more than the 2 percent, and the body is made again from a stream of its own; measured (GEN.986): the age rises by a median 4.3 Gyr in about 63 percent of rocky planets, and the class letter moves in 8 to 12 percent of those made again | planets.AGE_SETTLE_TOLERANCE | 0.02, fixed | rocky planets and moons | none needed |

### 6.13 Stand-ins of the tidal heating (GEN.737)

The tide of an eccentric orbit as a heat source (`physics/tidal_heating.py`, `physics/interior_heat.py`). The formula is Peale, Cassen and Reynolds 1979; the rheology (`k2/Q`) is where the stand-ins are. Kinds, tiers and tweak levels as in 6.1 and in the list of every stand-in. Enceladus is not reproduced: the equilibrium tide of its eccentricity is about 1 GW (Meyer and Wisdom 2007, recalled) and it radiates about 16 GW (Howett et al. 2011, recalled), a published open problem; the old 1 to 50 times resonance boost that papered over it (tier T9) is gone.

| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | evidence | constant | knobs | validity | replaced_by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TH1 | P | T2 | L0 | the shear modulus of the mantle in PREM runs from 65 to 80 GPa (recalled) | tidal_heating.ROCK_RIGIDITY_PA | 7e10, fixed | the solid mantle of a rocky body | a rigidity from the body's composition and temperature |
| TH2 | T | T2 | L0 | the Love number of a homogeneous, incompressible elastic sphere, k2 = (3/2) / (1 + 19 mu / (2 rho g R)) (Love 1911, recalled); it gives the Moon 0.021 against the observed 0.0242 | tidal_heating.rigid_k2 | rigidity only | a rocky body without a large liquid core | a layered Love number |
| TH6 | P | T2 | L0 | the peridotite liquidus lies 400 to 500 K above its solidus (Hirschmann 2000, recalled) | tidal_heating.MELT_RANGE_K | 450, fixed | a mantle between its solidus and its liquidus | a melt range from the composition |
| TH13 | P | T3 | L2 | the solidus runs from 1393 K at the surface through 1750 K at 3 GPa and 2200 K at 10 GPa (Hirschmann 2000, recalled) to 4180 K at the core-mantle boundary (Fiquet et al. 2010, recalled); the Simon-Glatzel curve fitted through the surface, 10 GPa and 135 GPa points gives 1750 K at 3 GPa without being fitted to it | tidal_heating.SOLIDUS_SURFACE_K, SOLIDUS_PRESSURE_SCALE_GPA, SOLIDUS_EXPONENT | 1393 K, 2.2 GPa, 0.267 | mantle pressures from 0 to 135 GPa, read at the middle of the mantle | a solidus from the composition of each body |
| TH8 | T | T6 | L0 | the tide is taken constant over the age at the stored eccentricity, though a planet that has circularised had more of it in the past | interior_heat.heat | constant, fixed | every body with a stored eccentricity | the eccentricity history |
| TH9 | T | T6 | L1 | a body whose tide is at least its own heat and leaves the mantle making more heat at the critical melt fraction than it can conduct is melt-limited: the melt erupts and carries out whatever is made, so the mantle holds the temperature of that melt fraction (the pipe has no capacity limit) and the tide is the rheology's there; the real pipe's flux is set by the eruption rate | interior_heat.heat | the two conditions | rocky bodies | a heat-pipe loss law with the eruption rate |
| TH10 | P | T3 | L0 | a gas giant's Love number 0.3 and quality factor 1e5 (Jupiter k2 0.49 and Q 3e4 to 1e6, Saturn 0.39 and 1.8e4 from the motions of their moons; recalled) | tidal_heating.GIANT_K2, GIANT_TIDAL_Q | 0.1 to 0.5 and 1e4 to 1e6 | gas giants | a Love number and Q from the interior model |
| TH11 | T | T6 | L0 | the tide that heats a body and sets its Q is the eccentricity tide, at the orbital rate; a free spinner's semi-diurnal tide, whose frequency is `2 (spin - n)`, is not in the heat (the spin's own torque sum, GEN.957, uses it) | tidal_heating.mean_motion_rad_s | orbital rate, fixed | rocky bodies with a stored eccentricity | the spin-dependent tide frequency |
| TH12 | T | T6 | L1 | the mantle temperature that sets a body's `k2` and Q in the spin and the circularisation is the box run without the tide on a stagnant lid at 273 K, so every user agrees without a stored column; the tide-warmed mantle is the box's own | interior_heat.tide_temperature_k | stagnant lid, 273 K, no tide | rocky bodies | a stored mantle temperature |

### 6.14 Stand-ins of the spin and its despinning (GEN.731)

The birth spin of a planet, the tide that wears it down and the resonances it can be caught in (`physics/spin.py`, `physics/tidal_spin.py`, `planets.generate_spin`). The rheology (`k2`, `Q`) is the one of 6.23, per body (`interior_heat.tidal_response`, GEN.955), and also circularises the orbit (OE10). Kinds, tiers and tweak levels as in 6.1 and in the list of every stand-in. The birth spin of a rocky planet was much faster than the Solar System's Earth and Mars (a median day of about 2 to 3 hours against 24 and 24.6); GEN.979 derives it from the mass ratio of the last impacts (Kokubo and Genda 2010 equation 4, `physics/impact_spin.py`, the rows of 6.26: a median free day of 0.1 to 5 Earth masses of about 7 hours, Mars-like days in half of the planets of its mass); the source simulates the last giant impacts only, and later impacts, the Moon's tide, a thick air column and the core-mantle coupling are not in this model.

| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | evidence | constant | knobs | validity | replaced_by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SP2 | T | T3 | L1 | the obliquity after a giant impact is isotropic, n(e) = sin(e)/2, which comes from components of one width (Kokubo and Genda 2010); a planet built by planetesimals alone spins prograde, the sense of the shear that feeds it (Schlichting and Sari 2007, recalled), so its z component is folded positive; the fold is the full effect where the paper finds a bias | spin.accretion_spin | isotropic or prograde, fixed | a rocky planet | a measured obliquity distribution |
| SP3 | T | T7 | L0 | the paper finds the mean spin independent of mass at one density; a planet of another density is scaled by its own breakup rate sqrt(G M / R^3), which is assumed, not read | spin.accretion_spin | scale by breakup, fixed | a rocky planet of any density | the paper's mass and density scan |
| SP4 | P | T6 | L0 | a birth spin is held at 0.85 of the breakup rate, the cap the hot stars use (planetGen's choice); about 14 percent of the Gaussian vectors lie above it | spin.BREAKUP_FRACTION | 0.7 to 1 | a rocky planet | a source on the spin of a planet at the end of accretion |
| SP5 | P | T3 | L1 | a moon's birth spin is log-uniform over 8 to 48 hours (the range this generator has used for rocky bodies since GEN.104, whose source was the impact papers), a gas giant's uniform over 8 to 20 hours (Jupiter 9.9, Saturn 10.7, Neptune 16.1, Uranus 17.2 h, recalled); the tide then moves both | constants.ROTATION_PERIOD_RANGE_HOURS | 8 to 48 and 8 to 20 | moons; gas giants | a circumplanetary-disk spin law for moons; a giant spin-up law |
| SP6 | P | T2 | L0 | the moment-of-inertia factor I / (m R^2): Earth 0.331, Venus 0.33, Mars 0.365, Mercury 0.346, the Moon 0.393 (rock, 0.33); Jupiter 0.254, Saturn 0.22 (giants, 0.25); recalled | tidal_spin.ROCKY_MOMENT_FACTOR, GIANT_MOMENT_FACTOR | 0.25 to 0.4 | rocky bodies; gas giants | a factor from the body's core fraction |
| SP7 | T | T3 | L0 | the despinning torque is the rheology's: `k2/Q` at the semi-diurnal frequency `2 (spin - n)` in magnitude of the body's own mantle (`spin_torques.rheology_dissipation` at `interior_heat.tide_temperature_k`, GEN.957), so a hot body despins fast and a cold stiff one slowly, and a gas giant keeps a fixed pair (`tidal_heating.GIANT_K2`, `GIANT_TIDAL_Q`) while ice has its shell's (`icy_tide.k2_over_q`, GEN.960); Gladman et al. 1996 (Icarus 122:166, recalled) is the constant-Q special case | body_spin.dissipation_of | none | a body spun down to the orbital rate | the layered-planet response with an ocean or a molten core |
| SP8 | P | T2 | L0 | READ through a summary of Makarov 2012 (ApJ 752:73, arXiv:1110.2658): with a frequency-dependent rheology capture is certain in 0.34 < e < 0.49 for 2:1, 0.2 < e < 0.41 for 3:2 and e < 0.29 for 1:1; the first window met coming down takes the spin | tidal_spin.RESONANCES | the window edges | a rocky planet brought down from a fast spin | the same windows for a layered planet |
| SP9 | P | T3 | L1 | capture inside a window is a chance (GEN.1172): 0.3 for the 3:2 and 0.1 for the 2:1, decided by two uniforms the body keeps from its birth spin; the sources split (Makarov 2012: 40 of 40; Goldreich and Peale 1966: 0.07 at Mercury's eccentricity with a constant-torque tide; Correia and Laskar 2004: 0.55 with the eccentricity's chaotic history), 3:2 is the middle of 0.07 and 0.55 rounded down and 2:1 a third of it, an educated guess from the sources; the synchronous state is caught for sure | tidal_spin.RESONANCE_CAPTURE_PROBABILITY | 0.07 to 1 | Mercury-like captures; captured moons with e of 0.2 to 0.5 | a rheology-dependent capture probability from one model |
| SP10 | T | T6 | L0 | approximations of the spin state: a locked body turns exactly once an orbit (not 1 + 6 e^2 of a constant-time-lag tide); the eccentricity is the stored one, not the larger past one; the obliquity stays as drawn until the spin has come all the way down, then is the Cassini-state tilt of SP22; the planet's air is a thermal tide (SP13) and the moons are in the sum from GEN.959, but there is no core-mantle coupling | planets.generate_spin | none | every planet and moon | a coupled spin, obliquity and eccentricity history |
| SP13 | T | T6 | L0 | the thermal tide of the air acts on a rocky planet with a stored surface pressure above 0 and starlight above 0; not on a moon (its planet holds it), an icy body or a gas giant (its air has no surface) | body_spin.thermal_tide_of | none | rocky planets with air | a thermal tide for a moon with air and for a giant's envelope |
| SP14 | T | T6 | L0 | an asynchronous balance of the tides is reported at obliquity zero, prograde (tilt 0) if the rate is positive and retrograde (tilt 180) if it is negative (Venus), whatever tilt it was born with | planets.generate_spin | none | planets held away from synchronism by the air | the obliquity history under the thermal tide |
| SP17 | P | T1 | L0 | READ in full, Beyer and White 2024 (arXiv:2408.02638): the Kraft break is centred at 6550 K with a width of about 200 K, from 6450 to 6650 K (uncertainty about 50 K on the limits), 1.32 to 1.41 solar masses; the 217 stars cooler than it have a mean v sin i of 6.7 km/s (standard deviation 3.6), the 40 hotter ones 63 km/s (standard deviation 35) | spin.KRAFT_BREAK_LOW_K, KRAFT_BREAK_HIGH_K | 6400 to 6700 K | a main-sequence dwarf of solar metallicity | a break by mass and metallicity (GEN.1163) |
| SP18 | P | T1 | L0 | the mean v sin i of the stars just hotter than the break, 63 km/s (SP17), deprojected by 4 / pi for random orientation (the mean of sin i): about 80 km/s, kept as the median equatorial speed at the top of the break | spin.HOT_STAR_BREAK_SPEED_KMS | 60 to 100 km/s | a dwarf of 1.41 solar masses or less above the break | a speed by mass from stars between the break and 1.6 solar masses |
| SP19 | P | T1 | L0 | READ, Zorec and Royer 2012 (A&A 537:A120, arXiv:1201.2052, Table 4, read from the paper's text): the 1.60 to 2.00 solar mass bin (191 stars) has a Maxwellian mode of the fast rotators of 160 km/s and a dispersion of 54 km/s, a mean of 2 / sqrt(pi) times the mode, 180 km/s; the mode rises to 238 km/s at 3.15 to 3.85 solar masses, which the model does not follow (hot stars of every mass above 1.8 keep 180 km/s with a log-normal width of 0.45, a planetGen choice wider than the paper's 54 / 160); their lowest bin starts at 1.6 solar masses | spin.HOT_STAR_SPEED_KMS, HOT_STAR_SPEED_SIGMA, HOT_STAR_FULL_SPEED_MASS_SOL | 160 to 200 km/s; width 0.3 to 0.45 | a dwarf of 1.8 solar masses or more | a speed rising with mass above 1.8 solar masses (the paper's Table 4 trend, about 46 km/s per solar mass in the mode) |
| SP20 | P | T6 | L0 | between the top of the break (1.41 solar masses, SP18's 80 km/s) and the first mass the paper covers (SP19) the median speed is interpolated, linear in ln v over mass; no source covers the stars of 1.41 to 1.6 solar masses (the break paper gives one mean for all hotter stars); the interpolation is a bridge between two read anchors, not fitted | spin.KRAFT_BREAK_TOP_MASS_SOL, HOT_STAR_FULL_SPEED_MASS_SOL, spin.hot_star_median_speed_kms | a straight line in v or in ln v | a dwarf of 1.41 to 1.8 solar masses | the same speed by mass from a sample of F and early A stars with masses |
