### 6.15 Stand-ins of the trapped radiation belts (GEN.749)

The belts a magnetised body holds and the dose rate an orbit takes inside them (`physics/radiation_belts.py`). The geometry follows from the dipole moment, the wind's pressure and the spin; the intensity is Earth's scaled, not derived, and is checked on Earth and Jupiter. Kinds, tiers and tweak levels as in 6.1 and in the list of every stand-in. Not in it: a tilted or offset field (the South Atlantic Anomaly), a multipole body, moons and rings that sweep the belts clean (Saturn's), the plasma a moon supplies (Io's torus), the solar cycle and storms, heavy ions, a shield other than 100 mil of aluminium.

| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | evidence | constant | knobs | validity | replaced_by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| RB1 | T | T3 | L0 | the outer edge of a wind-limited body is 0.75 of the magnetopause standoff, which puts Earth's at 7.3 (the outer belt ends at about 40 000 km; recalled); the standoff is the Chapman-Ferraro scaling of `magnetism.magnetopause_rp` | radiation_belts.WIND_EDGE_FRACTION | 0.6 to 0.9 | a body with a dipole in a stellar wind | a drift-shell model of where the magnetopause shadows the belts |
| RB2 | T | T4 | L0 | the outer edge of a spin-limited body is 9 times the synchronous distance, beyond which the plasma no longer turns with the body: Jupiter's synchronous distance is 2.24 radii and its belts reach about 20 (recalled; Saturn would be 16) | radiation_belts.ROTATION_EDGE_FACTOR | 7 to 12 | a fast rotator with a strong field | a centrifugal-balance model of the plasma sheet |
| RB3 | P | T3 | L1 | the electron belt peaks at 0.62 of the outer edge for a wind-limited body (Earth's peak at 4 to 5 against 7.3) and at 0.25 of it for a spin-limited one (Jupiter's peak inside Io's orbit, 5.9, against 20); recalled, two bodies fit | radiation_belts.WIND_PEAK_FRACTION, ROTATION_PEAK_FRACTION | 0.5 to 0.7 and 0.2 to 0.35 | the two limits | AE-9 and Divine-Garrett peak positions as functions of the field |
| RB4 | P | T3 | L1 | the inner proton belt peaks at 0.35 of the electron peak (Earth's at 1.5 to 2 against 4.5); recalled, one body fits | radiation_belts.INNER_PEAK_FRACTION | 0.3 to 0.45 | a body with air or a surface to make the albedo neutrons | a cosmic-ray albedo neutron decay (CRAND) source model |
| RB5 | D | T4 | L0 | the inner edge of a body with air is 45 scale heights above the surface, where the air stops a bouncing particle (Earth's belts begin near 400 km, 1.06 radii); the scale height is `R T / (M g)` at the surface temperature | radiation_belts.ATMOSPHERE_EDGE_SCALE_HEIGHTS | 35 to 60 | a body with air | a bounce-loss lifetime against the density profile |
| RB6 | P | T6 | L3 | the intensity of each belt at its peak is Earth's 3000 Gy per year behind 100 mil of aluminium (about 300 krad; recalled from the orders of AP-8 and AE-8 in the shielded orbit literature) times the dipole moment to the 0.8, which puts Jupiter's belt some 2700 times Earth's, inside the 1e2 to 1e5 the Galileo and Juno dose records span; the exponent was fitted to that range, not derived | radiation_belts.EARTH_OUTER_PEAK_GY_YR, EARTH_INNER_PEAK_GY_YR, INTENSITY_EXPONENT | 2000 to 6000 Gy; 0.5 to 1.2 | a dipole body in a quiet wind | AP-9 and AE-9, Divine-Garrett and the Juno and Galileo dose measurements |
| RB7 | T | T6 | L3 | the profile of a belt rises as the fourth power (electrons) or the second (protons) of the distance past the inner edge, falls as the sixth power of `L` past the peak and is cut to nothing at the outer edge; shapes chosen so Earth has a slot (a tenth of the peak at L 2.5) and Io to Europa is about a factor 10 (Galileo: 6.7; recalled) | radiation_belts.OUTER_RISE_EXPONENT, INNER_RISE_EXPONENT, FALL_EXPONENT, EDGE_EXPONENT | 2 to 6 | the main belts of a dipole | tabulated flux maps in `B` and `L` |
| RB8 | T | T6 | L0 | approximations: a dipole aligned with the spin; dose behind one aluminium thickness only; no solar-cycle, storm or longitude dependence; moons, rings and plasma tori are not in it | radiation_belts.belts_for | none | every body with a field | a coupled field, plasma and belt model |


### 6.47 Stand-ins of the cosmic-ray and particle doses and the ozone spread (GEN.746, GEN.747, GEN.748)

The cosmic-ray dose of a thin or absent air column (`physics/radiation.py`), the dose of the stellar particles (`physics/solar_particles.py`) and the temperature and spread of the ozone column (`datasets/ozone_uv.py`, `physics/atmospheric_radiation.py`, `physics/ozone.py`). Kinds, tiers and tweak levels as in 6.1 and in the list of every stand-in. Not in it: a cascade from the stellar particles (primaries only), the Carrington-class tail of the solar events (about 2 percent of the fluence, left out), a second star's events (every star scales the Sun's mean spectrum), a cosmic-ray dose that follows the solar cycle, and the ozone spread of any host but the Sun's (applied to all).

### 6.11 Stand-ins of the young-group draw (GEN.797)

Every number the young non-hierarchical group (GEN.327, GEN.328: `multistar.draw_young_group`) holds that is not a measurement has a row here, in the columns of 6.7: fill id, kind (D derived, B bracket, T theory, P placeholder, section 1), tier, tweak, evidence, the constant (`module.NAME`, in `planetgen.tuning` or `planetgen.generation.phenomena.system_types`), the knobs as the code holds them, the range inside which it is meant to hold, the test that stays red when the value stops doing what the row says (or why there is none), and what replaces it. The evidence column says what the repository has read: none of the papers behind the group figures is opened here (the design note 13.1 and 3.3 cite them from the catalogue), so no row is T1, and every row is at L0 because none was moved after the fact. A test (`test_multistar_standin_registry.py`) fails on a row whose constant or test does not exist, and on a constant of the draw with no row.

### 6.16 Stand-ins of the interior heat and the r-process uranium (GEN.721, GEN.735, GEN.759)

Rows for the stand-ins of the mobile-lid heat law, the mantle ceiling and the thorium to uranium ratio of a body's rock (`physics/interior_heat.py`, `physics/rprocess_enrichment.py`). Tiers and tweaks are the lane's reading (Aux 5), for the owner to confirm.

| IH1 | P | T6 | L0 | interior_heat.MOBILE_HEAT_FLOW_EXPONENT 1/3 (the mobile lid's Nusselt exponent beta) | 0.12 to 0.33 (O'Neill's runs 0.26 evolving mobile lid, 0.12 stagnant; Moresi and Solomatov 0.224 to 0.293 for a yielding lid) | rocky bodies with a mobile lid | a calibrated mobile-lid law that gives a different exponent at the same Rayleigh number | a mobile-lid law read from Korenaga or Driscoll and Bercovici | READ (Gurnis 1989 as quoted in O'Neill 2020, arXiv:2006.05654 section 1; boundary-layer theory gives 1/3) |
| IH2 | P | T4 | L0 | interior_heat.MOBILE_CRITICAL_RAYLEIGH 450 (the stagnant lid's value reused) | none | rocky bodies with a mobile lid | a published critical Rayleigh number for a mobile lid | the same calibrated law as IH1 | design (no mobile-lid value read; Thiriet et al. 2019 gives 450 for the stagnant lid) |
| IH3 | P | T6 | L0 | interior_heat.mantle_ceiling_k: the liquidus at the mid-mantle pressure of the dry body, `310.317 (p + 29.102)^0.5337` K with p in GPa | none | rocky bodies; a magma ocean in the first million years is held at the ceiling | a measured melt fraction of a young mantle well below or above the liquidus at mid-depth | a depth-integrated mantle temperature | READ (Manske et al. 2025, arXiv:2506.18718 Table 1 for the liquidus; the choice of mid-mantle pressure is the lane's) |

### 6.18 Stand-ins of the life constants (GEN.870)

Rows for the constants the life draw and the habitability index (`PHI_bio`, `PHI_cpx`, Suitability) read: the chemistries' gates and star weights, the stars' lifespans and evolutionary timelines, the score thresholds, the solvent factors, the toxicity limits, the oxygen band, the energy flux and its shares. Written from `reports/life-chemistry-review-2026-10-28.md` section 2.3 and the comments in the code. Tiers and tweaks are the review's proposal, for the owner of each area to correct. `tests/test_life_standin_registry.py` fails when a constant of the life code has no row here and no stated reason to be left out. The class tables (GEN.886, GEN.889, GEN.893) replaced the chemistry rows (LF1, LF2, LF4, LF53, LF55 and LF56) with a row for every class window and supply (section 6.19) and rows LF57 and LF58 for the production and complexity numbers.

### 6.19 Stand-ins of the life class rules (GEN.893)

One row for each life class of `src/planetgen/physics/data/life_classes.json` (GEN.886): its solvent factor, energy band and efficiency, gates, temperature and pressure windows and its supply placeholder, and the tier and tweak level of the least certain number in it (the rule file itself carries a tier and a tweak with the reason for every number). `src/tests/test_life_class_standin_rows.py` fails when a class has no row, when a row's tier or tweak is better than its weakest number, or when a rule-file number is not named by its row. Rows LF46 to LF48 are the thresholds and references the classes share.

### 6.20 Stand-ins of the planet mass law (GEN.711)

The planet mass law (`physics/planet_mass.py`, `docs/design/composition/planet-mass-law.md`) gives a planet its mass from the disc's solids at its formation distance, with the core-accretion rule that turns a heavy enough core into a gas giant. The numbers below are tuned by hand against the occurrence of planets (Aux 16's check, `reports/planet-class-distribution-check-2026-10-25.md`); the rates they are tuned to are recalled, not read. The law is wired into generation by the class cut-over (GEN.646); until then no generated value depends on these rows.

<!-- planet-mass-standins:begin -->
| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PM2 | P | T3 | L3 | tuning.PLANET_INNER_DISC_STEEPENING 0.75 (the solids rise faster than the nebula's -1.5 power inside 1 AU) | 0.5 to 1.0 | inside 1 AU | test_planet_mass::test_the_inner_disc_is_steeper_than_the_nebula_inside_one_au; an M dwarf occurrence of 1 to 20 Earth-mass planets inside 0.45 AU outside 1 to 2.5 | a pebble-drift model of the inner disc | RECALLED (Chiang and Laughlin 2013: minimum-mass extrasolar nebula about the -1.6 to -2 power; Dressing and Charbonneau 2015: about 2.5 planets of 0.5 to 4 Earth radii per M dwarf within 50 days); hand-tuned |
| PM5 | T | T3 | L0 | tuning.GIANT_CORE_CRITICAL_MASS_EARTH 10 (the core that can run away into a gas giant) | 5 to 20 | beyond the snow line | a measured critical core outside 5 to 20 Earth masses | a core-accretion model with the opacity of the envelope | RECALLED (Pollack et al. 1996; Rafikov 2006: 5 to 20 Earth masses) |
| PM8 | T | T5 | L1 | tuning.GIANT_NO_GAS_CORE_MAX_EARTH 20 (the core that finds no gas stops near an ice giant's mass) | 15 to 30 | beyond the snow line | test_planet_mass::test_a_core_that_finds_no_gas_stops_at_an_ice_giants_mass; a measured ice-giant mass function reaching above 30 Earth masses | a model of the late solid accretion of the outer disc | READ-level facts (Uranus 14.5 and Neptune 17.1 Earth masses) with a recalled microlensing occurrence (Suzuki et al. 2016) |
| PM9 | P | T7 | L0 | tuning.GIANT_RUNAWAY_MIN_MASS_EARTH 30 (a runaway giant is at least this heavy) | 20 to 60 | all hosts | a giant of runaway origin measured below 20 Earth masses | the giant mass function of the cores' own growth | design (the old floor of class J) |
<!-- planet-mass-standins:end -->

### 6.21 Stand-ins of the life timeline and the biogenic oxygen (GEN.750, GEN.855)

Rows BO1 to BO9 for the oxygen oxygenic life puts in the air (`physics/biogenic_oxygen.py`). The rows of the hard-step timeline of life (`physics/life_timeline.py`, `tuning.LIFE_*`) and of the gates of the light-harvesting chemistries are LF49 to LF56 in section 6.18, where the life constants are. Design note: `docs/design/habitability/life-timeline-and-oxygen.md`. Tiers T1 to T9 and tweak levels L0 to L4 as in 6.6a (GEN.725). Tiers and tweaks are the lane's reading (Core 1), for the owner to confirm. The Earth figures of BO1 to BO3 are the test point and the scale of the oxygen budget, not a preset: a world with another light, sea, heat, redox and gravity gets another oxygen.

<!-- life-timeline-standins:begin -->

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| BO2 | P | T3 | L3 | biogenic_oxygen.EARTH_REDUCTANT_MOL_YR 3e12: the reductant flux R at Earth | 1e12 to 5e12 | the oxygen balance | the same | the same | RECALLED (Holland 2002, a few 1e12) |
| BO3 | P | T9 | L3 | biogenic_oxygen.EARTH_STORE_MOL 5e21: the store of reductants that takes the first oxygen | 3e21 to 1e22 | the delay of the oxygen | the same (0.714 Gyr) | a measured Great Oxidation delay | needed output (the first oxygen followed the first oxygenic photosynthesis by 0.3 to 1 Gyr) |
| BO4 | P | T6 | L3 | biogenic_oxygen.LIGHT_EXPONENT 0.5 and LIGHT_RANGE (0, 2): production goes as the active light to this power | 0.3 to 1.0 | worlds with a sunlit sea | test_biogenic_oxygen.test_the_sea_makes_nothing_outside_its_liquid_range_and_most_near_the_reference | a measured productivity against light | design (production saturates) |
| BO5 | T | T2 | L0 | biogenic_oxygen.TEMPERATURE_ACTIVATION_EV 0.32: the Boltzmann factor on production | 0.2 to 0.7 eV | the liquid range | the same | the same | RECALLED (Allen et al. 2005, about half a heterotroph's) |
| BO6 | P | T6 | L3 | biogenic_oxygen.OCEAN_SPAN_K (258, 275, 315, 343): where the liquid range's factor rises to 1 and falls to 0 | a few K each | worlds with a sunlit sea | the same | a measured temperature range of marine production | design |

### 6.22 Stand-ins of the class cut-over (GEN.646)

The class cut-over (GEN.646) makes a body from its mass, where it formed and its star, and derives the class letter from what it comes out as. The numbers below are the choices that cut-over adds; the mass law's own are 6.16. They are tuned by hand against Aux 16's class distribution check (`reports/planet-class-distribution-check-2026-10-25.md`) and the sweep of `reports/class-cutover-sweep-2026-10-28.md`; the rates they are tuned to are recalled, not read.

It gives a Uranus-mass giant Neptune's 0.33 W/m2 where Uranus measures about 0.04, an unexplained anomaly of Uranus; the temperature test of the ice giants gives Uranus its measured heat so that the greenhouse is tested alone | the exponent 1.4 to 1.9 | giants | test_giant_one_bar_temperature::the cooling law gives a Uranus-mass giant far more heat than Uranus has | the cooling tracks of the radius tables with the heavy-element interior of each giant (GEN.1033) | RECALLED (Pearl and Conrath 1991: Neptune 0.43 W/m2, Uranus 0.042; Jupiter 5.4) |

### 6.23 Stand-ins of the tidal rheology (GEN.954)

The tidal Love number and quality factor from the mantle's temperature, melt and the tide's frequency (`physics/tidal_rheology.py`, read by `interior_heat.tidal_response` for the spin and the circularisation and by the heat box's tide, GEN.955). The form is Andrade (Efroimsky 2012, Castillo-Rogez et al. 2011) on the Maxwell rheology of Henning, O'Connell and Sasselov 2009, with the body's own drawn mantle viscosity. GEN.955 removed the fixed rock Q of 100 (TH3 now holds the ice's), the fitted molten `k2/Q` of Io (TH5) and the chosen melt share of the heat pipe (TH7, now TR2's critical melt fraction), and made the solidus a curve of the pressure (TH6, TH13).

<!-- tidal-rheology-standins:begin -->
| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TR1 | P | T2 | L0 | tidal_rheology.MELT_VISCOSITY_FACTOR 26 (a partly molten mantle is exp(-26 phi) times as viscous as its solid) | 20 to 31 | a rocky mantle between the solidus and the critical melt fraction | test_tidal_rheology::test_viscosity_is_arrhenius_then_weakened_by_melt_then_a_liquid_at_full_melt | a melt-fraction viscosity law that depends on the grain size and the melt's shape | Hirth and Kohlstedt 2003 (diffusion creep, 26) and Mei et al. 2002 (26 to 31), recalled, not opened |
| TR2 | P | T3 | L1 | tidal_rheology.CRITICAL_MELT_FRACTION 0.4 (the melt share at which the crystals stop touching and the mush becomes a suspension) | 0.2 to 0.5 | a rocky mantle | test_tidal_rheology::test_viscosity_is_arrhenius_then_weakened_by_melt_then_a_liquid_at_full_melt | a rheological transition that depends on the crystal size | the range in Arzi 1978 (0.2 to 0.4) and the 0.4 of Henning et al. 2009, recalled; the top of the range |
| TR3 | T | T6 | L3 | tidal_rheology.rigidity_pa and MELT_RIGIDITY_FLOOR 1e-3 (rigidity mu0 (1 - phi / phi_c)^2 down to a floor of a thousandth of the solid's) | floor 1e-4 to 1e-2 | a partly molten mantle | test_tidal_rheology::test_rigidity_falls_with_melt_to_a_floor | a shear modulus from the melt's geometry (Hashin-Shtrikman bounds) | physics fixes the form (a suspension has no static rigidity), the size is argued; the floor is the lowest value that keeps the Io state's k2 under the fluid 3/2 |
| TR4 | P | T2 | L0 | tidal_rheology.ANDRADE_EXPONENT 0.3 (the transient creep of grain boundaries) | 0.2 to 0.4 | a rocky mantle at tidal frequencies | test_tidal_rheology::test_the_moon_comes_out_with_the_lunar_laser_ranging_k2_and_q | the exponent from the grain size and the temperature | laboratory olivine 0.2 to 0.4; 0.3 in Jackson and Faul 2010 and Castillo-Rogez et al. 2011, recalled |
| TR5 | P | T6 | L0 | tidal_rheology.ANDRADE_TIMESCALE_RATIO 1 (the Andrade timescale over the Maxwell time) | 0.01 to 100 | a rocky mantle | test_tidal_rheology::test_the_moon_comes_out_with_the_lunar_laser_ranging_k2_and_q | the ratio measured for a mantle rock | 1 in Efroimsky 2012; Renaud and Henning 2018 explore 0.01 to 100; the Moon's Q of 38 to 60 is reproduced at 1 without tuning |
| TR6 | T | T6 | L0 | tidal_rheology.love_number (a homogeneous incompressible sphere with the complex rigidity: one temperature and one melt share stand for the whole mantle; the core, the cold lid and Io's thin warm layer are not separate) | none | any body that is mostly one material | test_tidal_rheology::test_the_solid_earth_has_the_semidiurnal_tide_of_a_q_of_a_few_hundred | a layered Love number (propagator-matrix) | the form of Love 1911 with the correspondence principle; the solid Earth's k2 comes out 0.5 against the real 0.30 |
| TR7 | T | T6 | L0 | tidal_rheology.viscosity_pa_s above CRITICAL_MELT_FRACTION (a straight line in log10 viscosity to a liquid's 1 Pa s at full melt) | none | a mush above the critical melt fraction | test_tidal_rheology::test_a_magma_ocean_tends_to_the_fluid_love_number_and_no_dissipation | a suspension law (Roscoe-Einstein) | the form is chosen to be continuous at phi_c; the end points are the solid's value there and a silicate liquid |

| TR11 | P | T7 | L0 | tidal_rheology.MAX_VISCOSITY_EXPONENT 200 (a numerical cutoff: the Arrhenius exponent `Ea / R T` the viscosity is evaluated at, a viscosity of 1e87 times the prefactor, beyond which a mantle does not relax in any tide and the exponential would overflow) | 100 to 600 | a mantle far below its solidus | test_tidal_rheology::test_a_mantle_far_colder_than_any_body_does_not_overflow_and_is_elastic_with_the_cold_ceiling | a numerical cutoff stays | the response of such a mantle is the elastic one with the cold ceiling on Q whatever the cutoff, so no value in the range changes an output |

### 6.24 Stand-ins of the spin torques (GEN.956)

The sum of the torques on a planet's spin: its star's tide, each moon's tide and the thermal tide of its air (`physics/spin_torques.py`, wired into `planets.generate_spin` by GEN.957 through `body_spin.py`). The gravitational torque takes the frequency-dependent `k2/Q` of 6.23 for rock (SP7 of 6.14) and a fixed pair for a giant and ice; the resonance windows are SP8 and SP9. The thermal torque is the Debye form of Leconte, Wu, Menou and Murray 2015 (Science 347:632, arXiv:1502.01952, read in full), whose amplitude is tabulated for four atmospheres only.

<!-- spin-torques-standins:begin -->
| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TQ1 | P | T3 | L0 | spin_torques.GRAVITATIONAL_TORQUE_FACTOR 3 (the built despinning's constant; Murray and Dermott and Leconte et al. write 3/2 for the same constant-Q result; the thermal torque is scaled with it so their ratio stays the paper's) | 1.5 or 3 | every tide | test_spin_torques::test_the_moon_slows_the_earth_by_the_measured_two_milliseconds_a_century | one definition of Q for the whole chain (GEN.957 decides with the rheology) | the 3 is Gladman et al. 1996 as SP7 has it; at 3/2 the lunar torque gives the Earth's measured 2.0 ms per century |
| TQ2 | P | T2 | L1 | spin_torques.THERMAL_TABLE (the thermal tide's pressure amplitude q0 and relaxation time 1/omega0 at 1, 10 and 92 bar, brought to a common flux) | the table | an Earth-like or Venus-like air at 1 to 92 bar | test_spin_torques::test_thermal_amplitude_and_relaxation_reproduce_leconte_table_1 | a scaling law for q0 and omega0 from the air's composition, pressure and heating (Auclair-Desrotour et al. 2017 give the shape only) | Table 1 of Leconte et al. 2015 (Venus 92 bar, 2610 W/m2: 201 Pa, 3.77e-7 /s; 1366 W/m2: 1180 Pa and 2.30e-6 /s at 1 bar, 4050 Pa and 1.46e-6 /s at 10 bar; 450 W/m2: 890 and 2960 Pa, 1.18e-6 and 7.17e-7 /s); the Venus row is moved to 1366 W/m2 with TQ3 |
| TQ3 | P | T6 | L2 | spin_torques.FLUX_EXPONENT_Q0 0.27 and FLUX_EXPONENT_OMEGA0 0.75 (q0 and omega0 against the stellar flux) | 0.2 to 0.35 and 0.6 to 0.9 | air at 450 to 2600 W/m2 | test_spin_torques::test_thermal_amplitude_and_relaxation_reproduce_leconte_table_1 | the paper's own climate runs at each flux | omega0 ~ F^(3/4) is the paper's; 0.27 fits its 1366 against 450 W/m2 at 1 and 10 bar (0.25 and 0.28) |
| TQ4 | P | T5 | L0 | spin_torques.THERMAL_SMALL_PRESSURE_SLOPE 1 (below 1 bar q0 goes with the surface pressure; the thermal time loses 6 percent of its surface part) | 0.8 to 1.2 | air under 1 bar | test_spin_torques::test_thin_air_has_a_proportionally_smaller_thermal_tide | a climate run for thin air | the paper's argument that the amplitude goes as the pressure over the scale height |
| TQ5 | T | T6 | L0 | spin_torques.Torques.atmospheric (the convective-atmosphere Debye torque, N_a = F K_a q0 x / (1 + x^2); a stably stratified air, which flattens it, is not modelled) | none | a thick air with a convective troposphere | test_spin_torques::test_the_asynchronous_equilibria_are_leconte_equation_13 | the stratified-air torque of Auclair-Desrotour, Mathis and Laskar 2017 | Leconte et al. 2015 equations 1 to 3; Auclair-Desrotour et al. 2017 equation 72 gives the same shape and say a stable stratification sends the torque to zero |
| TQ6 | T | T6 | L0 | spin_torques.evolve (a one-dimensional flow; a resonance whose window holds the eccentricity captures for sure, a sign flip of the torque at a raiser's orbital rate or a smooth zero stops it; obliquity zero, circular torques) | none | a planet with one star and up to a few moons | test_spin_torques::test_one_star_with_constant_q_is_the_built_despinning | a spin-orbit integration with obliquity and eccentricity | the same simplifications as SP10 of 6.14 |

### 6.26 Stand-ins of the birth spin from the last impacts (GEN.978)

The birth spin of a rocky planet from the mass ratio of its last impacts (`physics/impact_spin.py`, read by `spin.accretion_spin` since GEN.979). The formula is Kokubo and Genda 2010 equation 4 (read in full text); the distribution of the mass ratio by planet mass is not published in a form we hold, so it is a stand-in, built to cover the widest range of the two sources in question: Kokubo and Genda's 2 to 3 hours and Miguel and Brunini 2010's 10 to 10,000 hours. SP2 to SP4 of 6.14 stay the registry of the spin's axis, its scale by breakup and its cap; the width constants (SP1) and the interim scales (SP11, SP12) are gone.

<!-- impact-spin-standins:begin -->

| GS2 | P | T5 | L0 | impact_spin.MEAN_SIN2_THETA 0.5 (impact parameter uniform in area) | none | collisions in a disc | test_impact_spin::test_the_equation_reproduces_the_paper_s_own_rms_at_its_mass_ratio | the paper's measured angle distribution | geometry |
| GS3 | P | T6 | L2 | impact_spin.IMPACT_SPEED_RATIO 1.1 (the rms of impact speed over escape speed) | 1.0 to 1.3 | the last impacts of protoplanets | test_impact_spin::test_the_equation_reproduces_the_paper_s_own_rms_at_its_mass_ratio | the paper's mean of nu^2 | physics fixes the floor at 1 (a fall from rest at infinity); 1.1 gives the paper's rms 0.71 breakup at gamma 0.28 |
| GS4 | P | T6 | L3 | impact_spin.ISOLATION_MASS_EARTH 0.1 and ISOLATION_STEEPNESS 2 (the chance of a giant impact is 1 / (1 + (M_iso / M)^2)) | mass 0.05 to 0.2; steepness 1 to 4 | rocky planets of 0.01 to 10 Earth masses | test_impact_spin::test_mars_is_reachable_and_an_earth_mass_planet_seldom_turns_that_slowly | the distribution of the last impactor by planet mass from Kokubo and Ida 2007 or Agnor et al. 1999 | the isolation mass of oligarchic growth is 0.1 to 0.2 Earth masses at 1 AU (Kokubo and Ida 2000, recalled); the steepness is chosen so that Mars is as likely to have had a giant impact as not |

The dipole moment of a rocky core or a giant's metallic layer from the power that drives it and the spin (`physics/dynamo.py`): the buoyancy-flux scaling of Olson and Christensen (2006), the Rossby number that gates the dipole, and the share of a giant's heat that drives it. GEN.967 replaces the design defaults of `magnetism` with it.

### 6.31 Stand-ins of the accreting compact binaries (GEN.272, GEN.724)

The periods, donors and shares that `generation/multistar.py` draws for the X-ray binaries, microquasars and spider pulsars (`tuning.py`, the block that opens with the GEN.272 comment; design note `docs/design/systems/multi-star-systems.md` 13.4). Registered by GEN.724: `src/tests/test_accreting_standins.py` fails when a constant of that block has no row here. Kinds, tiers and tweak levels as in 6.1 and in the list of every stand-in. The registry row `millisecond-pulsar-companion` (`generation/phenomena/system_types.py`) carried 5 percent neutron-star companions for recycled pulsars; the code keeps 0.2 percent (Boss, 4:01 PM EDT 2026-10-24, card option 1) because 5 percent would be about ten times the double neutron stars the gravitational-wave rate allows, and the row now says so (AC10).

### 6.33 Stand-ins of the stellar spectra (GEN.970)

The spectra library (`datasets/stellar_spectra.py`, `physics/stellar_library.py`) reads two model-atmosphere grids (SL1, 6.6) and adds the chromosphere and corona from the eleven MUSCLES stars. Checked against the Sun's measured ultraviolet shares and against the near-ultraviolet of the eleven stars (`test_stellar_library_real.py`). The MUSCLES host (archive.stsci.edu) fails its TLS chain from the cloud machines, so the star-by-star fluxes are the paper's tables (France et al. 2016, Tables 1 and 3), not the HLSP files.

### 6.35 Stand-ins of the condensates by temperature (GEN.950)

`physics/condensate_shares.py` holds Lodders 2003 Tables 10 and 11 (the temperatures at which the ices condense and the mass of each condensate for the Solar System composition at 10^-4 bar) and gives the water share of the solids by disk temperature and by distance. The values are the paper's own; the open question is whether planetesimals follow nebular equilibrium (ice over rock about 2) or comets (about 1, Marboeuf et al. 2014, read second-hand in Emsenhuber et al. 2021), which the wiring step settles with the water-share switch of GEN.468.

<!-- condensate-standins:begin -->

| CS1 | P | T1 | L0 | condensate_shares.ICE_CONDENSATION_K (water 182 K, chosen by Boss at 10:05 PM EDT 2026-10-29 over the old 170 K, ammonia hydrate 131 K, methane 41 K; the methane clathrate at 78 K is left out) | the pressure: 10^-4 bar | the gas of the minimum-mass nebula near the snow line | a disk whose mid-plane pressure at the snow line differs by two decades or more and puts the water line more than 10 K away | a pressure-dependent condensation temperature (Fray and Schmitt 2009, held second-hand) | test_condensate_shares::test_the_water_share_steps_down_at_each_ice_line_as_table_11_prints |
| CS2 | P | T1 | L0 | condensate_shares.TABLE_11_MASS_PERCENT (rock 0.489, water 0.571, ammonia 0.097, methane 0.330, percent of the nebula, Solar System composition) | the composition column (photospheric gives the same ratios; Anders and Grevesse 1989 gives water over rock 2.09) | solar C/O, every carbon in methane and every nitrogen in ammonia | planetesimals whose ice over rock measured near 1 (comets) instead of 2.04 | the comet-calibrated ice budget (Marboeuf et al. 2014) as a switch | test_condensate_shares::test_the_water_share_steps_down_at_each_ice_line_as_table_11_prints |

### 6.36 Stand-ins of the planet architecture (GEN.728, GEN.729, GEN.730, GEN.828, GEN.829, GEN.920)

The planet architecture (`physics/planet_architecture.py`, `docs/design/composition/planet-architecture.md`) replaces the uniform planet count, the uniform spacing ratio and the constant 0.4 survival with a Kepler-like inner system drawn at a sourced share, a chain of oligarchs thinned by dynamical instability (Pu and Wu 2015), an outer chain at the isolation mass, a gas outcome and the migration of giants. The observed rates it is checked against are the sourced bands of `physics/data/planet_occurrence.json`, each READ or 2ND-HAND with its tier. Its own numbers are tuned together (the `ARCH_*` constants carry a copy of the mass law's disc and gas numbers, so the live law does not move until the generator is wired to the architecture). Tiers and tweaks are the lane's reading (Planets 1), for the owner to confirm.

### 6.37 Stand-ins of the brown dwarf ceiling (GEN.494)

`physics/substellar_ceiling.py` reads the Baraffe et al. 2015 (BHAC15) isochrones for the temperature of the hydrogen-burning limit mass at each age: the hottest a brown dwarf can be, about 2950 K at 10 Myr and falling to 2047 K at 1 Gyr and 1626 K at 10 Gyr. It gives the young late-M-like row (g1) of the atmosphere recipes its hot edge from a table instead of the 2800 K clip of the rogue bodies. The temperature map of Kirkpatrick et al. 2021 (Table 13) already gives the other edges (GEN.517, GEN.760).

<!-- substellar-ceiling-standins:begin -->
| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SB1 | P | T1 | L0 | substellar_ceiling.LIMIT_MASS_SOLAR 0.07 (the hydrogen-burning limit of the solar-metallicity models; the table also holds 0.072) | none | solar metallicity | a model whose limit lies more than 0.005 solar masses from 0.07 (the limit rises at low [Fe/H], GEN.936) | the limit as a function of [Fe/H] from the Baraffe table (GEN.936) | test_substellar_ceiling::test_the_limit_mass_is_hottest_at_ten_million_years_as_the_paper_says |
| SB2 | P | T1 | L0 | substellar_ceiling.HOTTEST_K 2950 (the highest temperature the limit mass reaches; the table has 2946 K at 10 Myr) | none | any age from 0.5 Myr to 10 Gyr | an isochrone set whose limit mass peaks more than 100 K from 2950 K | a later set of models | test_substellar_ceiling::test_the_limit_mass_is_hottest_at_ten_million_years_as_the_paper_says |
<!-- substellar-ceiling-standins:end -->

### 6.38 Stand-ins of the aluminium-26 and iron-60 at birth (GEN.476)

`physics/birth_radioisotopes.py` builds the ratio of aluminium-26 and iron-60 to their stable partners at a star's birth from the Galaxy's gamma-ray masses of the two (the yield per mass of stars formed), the depletion time of the star's region and the granularity of the sources, replacing the log-uniform 0.1 to 10 times the Solar System's own 5.23e-5 of `radiogenic`. The Solar System ratio (and the iron-60 ratio of 1.0e-8) are test points only.

<!-- birth-radioisotopes-standins:begin -->

| BR1 | P | T1 | L0 | birth_radioisotopes.MEAN_LIFE_YR (26Al 1.035 Myr, 60Fe 3.78 Myr) | none | any region | a measured half-life differing by more than 5 percent | the next evaluation of the half-lives | test_birth_radioisotopes::test_the_yields_per_mass_formed_are_the_gamma_ray_mass_over_rate_and_mean_life |
| BR2 | P | T1 | L0 | birth_radioisotopes.GALAXY_AL26_MASS_SOLAR 2.0 and FE60_AL26_FLUX_RATIO 0.15 (the gamma-ray masses of the two isotopes in the Galaxy; the 26Al mass is 1.7 to 3) | 26Al mass 1.7 to 3 | massive-star nucleosynthesis at about solar metallicity | a gamma-ray mass differing from 2.0 by more than 40 percent | a later INTEGRAL mass or a yield model (Limongi and Chieffi) | test_birth_radioisotopes::test_the_mean_ratio_is_about_ten_times_under_the_solar_systems |
| BR3 | P | T1 | L0 | birth_radioisotopes.GALAXY_STAR_FORMATION_SOLAR_MASSES_PER_YR 1.65 (the Galaxy's star formation rate, +-0.19) | 1.46 to 1.84 | the rate the gamma-ray masses are divided by | a rate differing by more than 20 percent | a later meta-analysis | test_birth_radioisotopes::test_the_yields_per_mass_formed_are_the_gamma_ray_mass_over_rate_and_mean_life |
| BR4 | P | T2 | L0 | birth_radioisotopes.RECURRENCE_RANGE_YR 5 to 10 Myr (the time between core-collapse additions to a region's gas, as Meyer and Clayton give it, read second-hand in Lugaro et al. 2018) | 5 to 10 Myr | a region with cluster formation at the Galactic rate | a cluster formation rate that puts the recurrence outside 3 to 15 Myr | a recurrence from the region's own cluster formation rate | test_birth_radioisotopes::test_the_draws_keep_the_mean_and_are_spiky_for_the_short_lived_isotope |

### 6.39 Stand-ins of the X-ray saturation time by mass (GEN.493)

`physics/saturation_time.py` gives the age at which a star falls out of X-ray saturation by mass (0.1 to 1.2 solar masses) and initial rotation percentile (the 5th, 50th and 95th of the 150 Myr distribution), read off Fig. 10 of Johnstone, Bartel and Guedel 2021. At one solar mass it matches Tu et al. 2015 (5.2, 22.8 and 297 Myr against 5.7, 23 and 226). It replaces the one set of solar-mass times that row p6 used for every mass.

<!-- saturation-time-standins:begin -->
| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| RS1 | P | T3 | L0 | saturation_time.ANCHORS_MYR (thirteen masses by three percentiles, read off the published figure by pixel position; the fast curve at 0.1 solar masses by eye) | each value within about 10 percent; the steep 1.1 to 1.2 solar mass edge of the fast curve within 30 percent | solar metallicity, 0.1 to 1.2 solar masses | a star sample with a saturated fraction against age and mass differing from the model's by more than a third (Medina 2022 gives 2.4 +- 0.3 Gyr for 0.1 to 0.3 solar masses, and the tests keep the median curve near it) | the tracks of the model itself (Johnstone's MIT-licensed code) or a measured saturated fraction against mass | test_saturation_time::test_the_published_curves_are_reproduced_at_their_anchors; test_saturation_time::test_the_mid_to_late_m_dwarfs_stay_saturated_for_gigayears_as_medina_2022_finds |
<!-- saturation-time-standins:end -->

### 6.41 Stand-ins of the derived taxon kernel (GEN.473)

`physics/taxon_kernel.py` derives the taxon weights of a belt from the disk temperature it formed at (the Lodders 2003 ice lines of `condensate_shares`) and from the aluminium-26 its star was born with (`birth_radioisotopes`), in place of the Solar System's zone table in `small_body_composition.ZONES`. The families agree with Table 6 to about 0.16 (rms of the share by zone), a test and not an input.

### 6.43 Stand-ins of the mass left in a belt (GEN.477)

`physics/belt_mass.py` gives a planetesimal belt its mass from the disc's solids ground down by collisions (Wyatt et al. 2007, Eqs. 14 and 15), in place of the uniform draw of the density word and the zero mass of NAV.62. The wiring took the belt's largest body `Dc` from LB1, a range drawn per belt, until GEN.1000 (done) drew it from the size law; the density word is the belt's mass per area against the same belt with the median `Dc`. The Solar System's belt (0.6 thousandths of an Earth mass inferred) is a test point: the model leaves 0.5 to 2.6 thousandths for Ceres-size largest bodies.

<!-- belt-mass-standins:begin -->
| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BM1 | P | T1 | L0 | belt_mass.GRAIN_DENSITY_KG_M3 2700 and STRENGTH_FLOOR_J_PER_KG 200 (the nominal planetesimal density and the strength of km-size planetesimals in Wyatt et al. 2007; the strength of bodies above about 100 km is the generator's gravity-bound Leinhardt and Stewart threshold) | density 2000 to 3500; strength 100 to 500 | belts of rock-rich planetesimals in collisional equilibrium | a measured belt mass more than a factor of 10 from the law at its age | a size-dependent strength law with the strength regime of Benz and Asphaug (1999, not read) | test_belt_mass::test_wyatts_equation_17_is_reproduced_at_its_stated_assumptions; test_belt_mass::test_the_solar_systems_belt_comes_out_within_a_few_times_the_inferred_mass |
| BM2 | P | T9 | L4 | belt_mass.DENSE_FACTOR 3 (a belt with three times the reference mass per area is dense, a third sparse) | 2 to 10 | all belts | the dense and sparse shares of the generated belts outside what the words are meant to cover | an observed spread of belt masses at one age | test_belt_mass::test_the_density_word_follows_the_mass_per_area_against_its_reference |
<!-- belt-mass-standins:end -->

### 6.44 Stand-ins of the chondrite-group map (GEN.475)

`physics/meteorite_groups.py` holds the chondrite groups as points of a four-axis composition space (olivine fayalite, metal, refractory inclusions and matrix, Weisberg et al. 2006 Table 1) and labels a body with its nearest group, so the group becomes an output of the grains a belt or field is drawn with (`small_body_composition.chondrite_group`, stored as `chondrite_group`). The taxa themselves stay inputs of the draw: the map names the group of a mix but gives no within-family shares, so TK6 stays a stand-in.

### 6.45 Stand-ins of the rock types from bulk oxides (GEN.474)

`physics/rock_types.py` works out the ground type of a body from the oxides of its rock: the CIPW norm, the melt of a mantle by stages, and the silica bands of the total alkali-silica diagram. The Earth's oxide analyses (bulk silicate Earth, the three crust layers) and a lunar highland anorthosite are test points, never inputs. The wiring (RT4, MF1) takes the volcanic crust of a body from the melt of its stored mantle: the oxides come from the stored FeO, Al2O3 and Mg/Si and the star's Ca/Mg, the melt share from the heat flow, and a melt the staged allocation gives as pure plagioclase reads basaltic (the stored rock has no alkalis, so a calcium-poor mantle's first melt is feldspar alone; an anorthosite is a flotation cumulate, not a mantle melt).

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

### 6.46 Stand-ins of the comet ice budget (GEN.595)

`physics/comet_ice_budget.py` gives the CO2/H2O and CO/H2O of a comet's ice from the zone of the disc it formed in (the snow lines and the carbon and oxygen budget of Oberg, Murray-Clay and Bergin 2011), in place of the Solar System's measured population as the within-system shape. 2I/Borisov's CO/H2O (1.30 to 1.55) is a test of the CO zone; the Solar System's median comet and 3I/ATLAS's CO2/H2O of 7.6 are not reproduced. The wiring (GEN.595, CI6) has every comet of a star's system form at a distance between the star's snow line and the edge of its disc and take its median CO and CO2 from the zone, with the population's scatter; the interstellar bracket (3I/ATLAS) stays a registered stand-in in `comet_population`.

### 6.53 Stand-ins of the debris disks (GEN.787)

The cold debris belt of a main-sequence star (`physics/debris_disk.py`, `tuning.py` block that opens with the GEN.787 comment): the share of stars of each kind that hold one, the temperature of the dust, and the ring it makes. Source: Sibthorpe et al. 2018 (MNRAS 475, 3046; arXiv:1803.00072), the Herschel DEBRIS survey, read 2026-10-30 through a fetched copy that held its first part verbatim (the abstract, sections 2.5 and 3, Tables 1 and 2 as counted). The mass of a disk is not drawn: only the light of its dust is measured.

### 6.56 Stand-ins of the registered rows that rest on a Solar System body (GEN.1002, GEN.1005)

### 6.57 Stand-ins of the magma-ocean redox model (GEN.1005)

`physics/magma_ocean_redox.py` gives the oxygen fugacity a magma ocean leaves at its surface from the pressure at its base: the base pressure from the mass (a stand-in power law), the ferric share of the iron from the pressure, and dIW from the ferric ratio. It replaces the line through three Solar System bodies that rows a1 and a2 of the atmosphere table drew from; those three bodies are now tests of the model, never inputs. Design: `docs/design/composition/magma-ocean-redox-model.md`. Part 1 is lock-free (the module, these rows, the expression functions `magma_ocean_diw`, `magma_ocean_diw_sigma` and `later_mantle_diw`); part 2 has rows a1 and a2 call them and retires SSR1 and SSR2.

### 6.58 Stand-ins of the dry-body carbon (GEN.1005)

`physics/dry_carbon.py` derives the carbon a dry rocky body keeps in its mantle and air (row b1 of the atmosphere table) from the carbon of its non-carbonaceous building blocks, the share that had cored before accretion and the share of the carbon the core leaves in the mantle: 0.029 to 0.108 weight percent CO2, against the 0.018 to 0.1 b1 drew, tuned to the bulk silicate Earth. The Earth is a test of the bracket, no longer its anchor. Design: `docs/design/composition/dry-carbon-model.md`. Part 1 is lock-free; part 2 has `mixture_rocky.INVENTORY['co2_dry_wt_percent']` read the bracket and retires SSR3.

| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DC1 | P | T1 | L0 | dry_carbon.NC_UNDIFFERENTIATED_C_PPM 1600 and NC_DIFFERENTIATED_C_PPM 40 (carbon of an undifferentiated and of a differentiated non-carbonaceous building block, ppm by weight) | none (the table's values) | a dry rocky body built from non-carbonaceous material | a carbon content of non-carbonaceous bodies measured outside 0.1 to 0.2 weight percent (undifferentiated) or above 0.01 (differentiated) | a building-block model by formation zone and star (GEN.1005 later part) | Blanchard et al. 2022 (EPSL, arXiv:2202.06809) Table 2, READ: 0.16 and 0.004 weight percent; test_dry_carbon::test_a_body_that_took_only_undifferentiated_material_holds_what_the_table_says |
| DC2 | P | T7 | L1 | dry_carbon.DIFFERENTIATED_SHARE 0.30 to 0.60 (the share of the accreted mass that had cored before it was accreted) | 0.15 to 0.75 | a dry rocky body accreted from planetesimals | a body-by-body model of the planetesimals' differentiation whose share lies outside 0.15 to 0.75 | an accretion model by formation zone (GEN.1005 later part) | Blanchard et al. 2022: 45 +/- 15 percent reproduces the mantle carbon with 3.35 weight percent carbonaceous carbon, 25 +/- 15 with 2.2; one accretion model of one planet; test_dry_carbon::test_the_mantle_share_and_the_differentiated_share_are_ordered_brackets_inside_zero_and_one |
| DC3 | P | T7 | L2 | dry_carbon.MANTLE_SHARE 0.12 to 0.26 (the share of a body's carbon left in the mantle and air after core formation) and CO2_PER_C 3.664 | 0.05 to 0.5 | a dry rocky body with a core | a metal-silicate partitioning model with core size, ocean depth and oxygen fugacity that puts the mantle share of a body outside 0.05 to 0.5 | a core-formation model with the partitioning of Blanchard et al. 2022 Eq. 4 (GEN.1005 later part) | Blanchard et al. 2022: 140 +/- 40 ppm in the mantle of 495 +/- 125 ppm of the whole, mantle 0.68 of the mass; carbon is not extracted efficiently because only 0.16 to 9.9 percent of the mantle equilibrates with each impactor core; test_dry_carbon::test_the_carbon_partitioning_of_the_source_gives_a_siderophile_element_that_the_core_still_leaves_in_the_mantle |

### 6.59 Stand-ins of the comet species (GEN.1005)

`physics/comet_species.py` writes the N2, Ar, OCS and SO2 of a comet's ice as the host star's own nitrogen, argon and sulfur per carbon over the nebula's carbon in CO, times a trapping share and the population's CO/H2O, and keeps O2, Kr and Xe at one value; the width is 0.4 dex where row k7 drew 0.6 by design. The shares and the three values are tuned to the one comet measured, so one comet (67P) is still the calibration of seven species: rows CK1, CK2 and CK5 name the item that replaces it. Design: `docs/design/composition/comet-species-model.md`.

| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CK1 | P | T7 | L3 | comet_species.TRAPPED_OVER_NEBULAR N2 0.158 and Ar 0.0145 (the share of the nebular amount per CO, times CO/H2O, that is in the comet's ice) | 0.05 to 0.5 for N2; 0.005 to 0.05 for Ar | a comet that formed beyond the CO snow line | N2 or Ar per water measured in a second comet that departs from the chain at its host's ratios by more than 0.4 dex | GEN.1021 | tuned to the one comet measured (Rubin et al. 2019: N2 8.9e-4, Ar 5.8e-6 per water); Bar-Nun: trapped gas drops six decades from 25 to 100 K, so the share depends on the formation temperature the generator does not carry; test_comet_species::test_a_host_of_solar_composition_gives_the_calibration_comet_within_two_percent |
| CK2 | P | T7 | L3 | comet_species.TRAPPED_OVER_NEBULAR OCS 0.187 and SO2 0.592 (the share of the nebular sulfur per CO, times CO/H2O, in the ice, for each) | 0.05 to 1 each | a comet with sulfur species | OCS or SO2 per water measured in a second comet that departs from the chain by more than 0.4 dex | GEN.1021 | tuned to the one comet measured (Rubin et al. 2019: OCS 4.1e-4, SO2 1.3e-3 per water); the sulfur of the two plus the population's H2S exceeds the nebular sulfur the chain allows, so the sulfur is not conserved across rows; test_comet_species::test_sulfur_species_follow_the_hosts_sulfur_and_argon_its_argon |
| CK3 | P | T6 | L1 | comet_species.carbon_in_co_share 0.52 (1.5 per 10^4 hydrogen in CO over the proto-solar carbon, comet_ice_budget.NEBULA_PER_1E4_H and tuning.STELLAR_SOLAR_LOG_EPS) | 0.3 to 0.7 | a host of any composition | a disc whose carbon in CO is measured outside 0.3 to 0.7 of its carbon | a disc chemistry model (GEN.1021) | Oberg, Murray-Clay and Bergin 2011 Table 1 (1.5 per 10^4 H, READ) over the proto-solar carbon of Asplund, Amarsi and Grevesse 2021; test_comet_species::test_the_carbon_in_co_is_half_of_the_nebulas |
| CK4 | P | T3 | L2 | comet_species.CO_OVER_H2O_MEDIAN 10^-1.602 (the median CO/H2O of the comets of the population, row k5) | 10^-1.602 plus or minus 0.16 dex | comets at heliocentric distance up to 2.5 AU | a population median of CO/H2O outside 10^-1.76 to 10^-1.44 | the row k5 refit (GEN.520) | Robinson et al. 2024 database, per-comet medians of 16 comets (GEN.520, comet-composition-tuning.md); test_comet_species::test_a_host_of_solar_composition_gives_the_calibration_comet_within_two_percent |
| CK5 | P | T7 | L3 | comet_species.UNSCALED_OVER_H2O O2 3.1e-2, Kr 4.9e-7, Xe 2.4e-7 per water (no host ratio) | each by 0.4 dex | a comet or asteroid of any formation temperature | the same species measured in a second comet that differ from 67P by more than 0.4 dex | GEN.1021 | one comet, 67P (Rubin et al. 2019); O2 is not tied to the oxygen budget by any source read; krypton and xenon are not in the abundance table; test_comet_species::test_o2_krypton_and_xenon_have_no_host_ratio_and_keep_one_value |
| CK6 | P | T6 | L1 | comet_species.SIGMA_DEX 0.4 (the width of every species, decades) | 0.33 to 0.6 | the seven species of row k7 that the chain covers | a spread of a species across comets measured outside 0.2 to 0.8 dex | GEN.1021 | Bar-Nun: the trapped gas drops six decades between 25 and 100 K (a decade per 12 K) and a comet's formation temperature is known to 5 K (48 +/- 5 K for Halley's CO); the four species a population measures scatter 0.33 to 0.39 dex; test_comet_species::test_the_width_sits_inside_the_scatter_of_the_four_species_a_population_measures_and_the_old_design_width |

### 6.164 Stand-ins of the mass ratio of a companion (GEN.951)

Rows MR1 to MR4. The mass ratio of another star of a system is drawn from the law of Moe and Di Stefano (2017) by primary mass and period, with a twin excess, not flat (`generation/mass_ratio.py`, `docs/design/galaxy/stellar-mass-function.md`). Tiers T1 to T9 and tweak levels L0 to L4 as in 6.6a (GEN.725); the lane's reading (Bug 1), for the owner to confirm.

| MR1 | D | T1 | L0 | tuning.MASS_RATIO_TWIN_EXCESS, MASS_RATIO_LARGE_Q_SLOPE, MASS_RATIO_SMALL_Q_SLOPE at log P 1, 3, 5, 7 for the five primary masses of `COMPANIONS_PER_DECADE_BY_PRIMARY_MASS`, with the break at q = 0.3 and the twins over 0.95 to 1 | the table's 1 sigma: slopes 0.3 to 0.6, twin fractions 0.03 to 0.09 | primaries of 0.8 to 40 solar masses, the zero-age main sequence | the generated companions' mass ratios at one period against the table's slopes and twin fraction | a later survey of mass ratios by period and primary mass | READ (Moe and Di Stefano 2017, ApJS 230:15, Table 13, opened in arXiv:1606.05347v2; Eqn. 2 and Table 1 for the form) |
| MR2 | P | T3 | L1 | tuning.MASS_RATIO_TWIN_EXCESS: the table's upper limits "F_twin < 0.03" (1 sigma) are read as no twin excess | 0 to 0.03 | log P of 3 and over for early-type primaries, 5 and over for A and late-B ones, 7 for solar-type ones | a survey that finds twins at wide separations of massive stars | a measured twin fraction there | an upper limit read as zero, inside the limit |
| MR3 | P | T9 | L0 | generation.mass_ratio: the law of the companion is the mix of the four periods' laws, each in the share of the primary's companions at that period (`COMPANIONS_PER_DECADE_BY_PRIMARY_MASS`), because the draw puts the masses before the periods; the link between one companion's mass ratio and its period is lost | none | the average over the companions of a primary mass | the generated mass ratio of the companions inside a period range against Table 13's row for that period | drawing the period before the mass | the order of the draw in `multistar`; the average over periods is the table's |
| MR4 | P | T6 | L0 | generation.mass_ratio: below the 1 solar mass row (the table's solar-type bin is 0.8 to 1.2) the solar-type row is held, and the O-type row is held above its 25 solar mass node | none | primaries under 0.8 solar masses, over 40 | the mass ratios of M dwarf companions (Winters et al. 2019; Janson et al. 2012) against the solar-type row | a table of M dwarfs by period | an inference: M dwarf companions are near flat in q, as the solar-type row is |

### 6.48 Stand-ins of the star counts' composition (GEN.661)

The number of stars a population puts in a mass band or above a luminosity (`generation/star_population.py`) depends on metallicity: a metal-poor star of one mass is brighter and hotter, so more of a metal-poor population is bright. The counts are worked out once for the whole population at the composition the population holds on average (`reference_fe_h`), not star by star. Each star's own birth [Fe/H] is drawn separately and does follow its component.

<!-- star-count-standins:begin -->
| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SCC1 | D | T5 | L0 | star_population.reference_fe_h: a population's counts are worked out at its mean [Fe/H] at the Sun's distance (the disc has no radial gradient here, a component has no value of its own in the counts) | none | the bright fraction is nearly linear in [Fe/H] over the range of a population (25 to 45 percent between 0 and -1.5 at 500 Lsun), so the fraction at the mean stands for the mixture | a population whose spread is wide enough that the fraction at the mean differs from the mean of the fractions by more than the count noise | counts worked out per component at its own [Fe/H] and per disc radius | COMPUTED (the bright fraction of the isochrone tables at [Fe/H] 0 and -1.5, test_star_population) |
<!-- star-count-standins:end -->

### 6.51 Stand-ins of the stars after the main sequence (GEN.935)

A star's luminosity, temperature and radius after the main sequence are read from the MIST v1.2 tracks (`physics/post_main_sequence.py`, `docs/design/galaxy/post-main-sequence-stars.md`, tier T1 data), so none of the old hand-set ranges is left. What stays a choice is when each phase is entered and left, where the mass boundaries between the kinds of ending lie, and what the instability strip and Yerkes classes are called. Kinds, tiers and tweak levels as in 6.1.

<!-- post-main-sequence-standins:begin -->

### 6.52 Stand-ins of the stellar [X/Fe] refit to Brewer and Fischer (GEN.988, GEN.989, GEN.990, GEN.991)

`tuning.STELLAR_XFE_FITS`, `STELLAR_XFE_SIGMA` and `STELLAR_XFE_CORRELATION` were fitted to Bensby 2014 and Nissen 2014 (the means, with Brewer and Fischer 2016 only for the residual correlation of 844 dwarfs). The refit holds them to the full table of 1,615 dwarfs (`scripts/fit_stellar_xfe_refit.py`, bands in `src/tests/data/brewer_fischer_xfe_bands.json`, test `test_stellar_xfe_brewer_fischer.py`). Brewer and Fischer's abundances are differential to the Sun, so a solar-metallicity thin-disc star sits 0.06 dex under the Sun in Mg and 0.03 in Si and in O; the thin plateaus follow.

### 6.65 Stand-ins of the arm contrast of each stellar population (GEN.931)

Rows ARM1 to ARM4. The single hand value of 0.0 for the old disk and the unsourced 0.9 and 0.4 for the young and intermediate populations (`tuning.STELLAR_POPULATION_ARM_AMPLITUDE`) are replaced by values derived from the gas ratio and the Kennicutt-Schmidt index (young), the observed old disk (Eilers 2020; Drimmel and Spergel 2001), and a fall with the log of the age between them (intermediate). Effect at the reference inter-arm place: the young share of the mix 0.12% to 0.32%, A stars 0.087% to 0.103%, B stars 0.013% to 0.020% (30,000 draws each); the A-star deficit is the star formation history's (GEN.898), not the arms'. Tiers T1 to T9 and tweak levels L0 to L4 as in 6.6a (GEN.725); the lane's reading (Bug 1), for the owner to confirm.

<!-- arm-contrast-standins:begin -->
| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ARM1 | P | T1 | L0 | tuning.ARM_CONTRAST_OLD_DISK 0.10: the old disk's arm contrast `A` in `1 + A cos(arm phase)` | 0.09 to 0.14 | old disk stars (red giants, near-infrared light) near the solar radius | an arm to inter-arm surface density ratio outside 1.2 to 1.35 for an old sample at the solar radius | a contrast by age from Gaia samples of A, F and G stars when one is published | READ (Eilers et al. 2020, ApJ 900:186, abstract: a surface mass density contrast of about +-10% at the solar radius; Drimmel and Spergel 2001, ApJ 556:181, section 4.3: arm to inter-arm 1.2 in J and 1.32 in K; Siebert et al. 2012: 14%) |
| ARM2 | D | T5 | L0 | tuning.ARM_GAS_TO_INTERARM_RATIO 4.0 raised to tuning.STAR_FORMATION_LAW_EXPONENT 1.4, as an amplitude (the young population's `A`, 0.749) | gas ratio 2 to 5 (A 0.45 to 0.80) | stars under 0.1 Gy, which have not left the gas they formed in | an arm to inter-arm ratio of young upper main sequence stars outside 3 to 12 | a number from the Gaia maps of young stars (Poggio et al. 2021 give maps and no number) | RECALLED (Koda et al. 2009, ApJ 700:L132: CO arm to inter-arm contrasts of about 2 to 5; Kennicutt 1998 index 1.4, a surface-density law applied here to the volume ratio) |

Rows LM1 to LM4 and LM6 (LM5, the fade of MIST's stretch, went with the stretch when the clock became the radius-settled time, GEN.1174). The main sequence under the MIST grid (0.07 to 0.1 solar masses) is the Baraffe et al. 2015 table's, and a brown dwarf's temperature and radius are the same table's, at its age (they were a power-law extension and eleven thinned rows). Tiers T1 to T9 and tweak levels L0 to L4 as in 6.6a (GEN.725); the lane's reading (Bug 1), for the owner to confirm.

### 6.60 Stand-ins of the multiple-star limits and the white-dwarf planets (GEN.1018)

The multi-star design (`design-drafts/overlap-free-systems-gen1006-2026-10-30.md`, GEN.1006) rests on constants of `generation/multistar.py`, `orbits` and `mass_loss` and on two stability margins it adds. GEN.1018 read the sources (report `reports/gen1018-multistar-stability-2026-10-30.md`): Mustill and Villaver 2012 in full text, Eggleton 1983, Domingos 2006, Mardling and Aarseth 2001, Eggleton and Kiseleva 1995 and Chambers 1996 second-hand, and the 2023 quadruple models of Vynatheya, Mardling and Hamers. GM7 and GM8 are not built yet: their constant cell names no module, so the wiring guard does not look for a reader.

### 6.61 Stand-ins of the potassium depletion by body class (GEN.492)

`physics/potassium_depletion.py` replaces the log-uniform 0.03 to 1 of `tuning.RADIOGENIC_K_DEPLETION_RANGE` (row o3 of the atmosphere recipes, RA11) with the K/U of six bodies in Dauphas et al. 2022 Table 1 (READ), over CI chondrites from Barrat et al. 2012 (K/U 71,429): Earth 0.136, Mars 0.243, Mercury 0.200, Moon 0.034, Vesta 0.058, the angrite parent body 0.0018. Report `reports/gen492-potassium-depletion-2026-10-30.md`.

### 6.62 Stand-ins of the carbon dioxide far wing and the sulfuric acid cloud (GEN.1019)

`physics/co2_far_wing.py` is the opacity the k-tables' 25 cm-1 line cutoff leaves out of a dense carbon dioxide air: the Lorentz tail of every band's lines times the sub-Lorentzian factor of Perrin and Hartmann 1989, out to a reach, with the band strengths read off the carbon dioxide k-table itself. It replaces the flat wing of IR3 (`CO2_FAR_WING_M2_KG`) when `line_opacity` calls it. The chi parameters are the HELIOS-K files `chiPH89__CO2-CO2.dat` and `chiPH89__CO2-N2.dat` (read 10-30); the paper was not opened. `physics/sulfuric_cloud.py` is the sulfuric acid cloud of an air that holds sulfur dioxide, an oxidiser and water: a base where the dry adiabat is cooler than 367 K, a depth drawn from the 20 to 30 of the published Venus cloud layers and a droplet radius from its published modes, held to the acid the sulfur above the base can make; an air with no sulfur has none. Report `reports/gen1019-co2-wing-venus-cloud-2026-10-30.md`. Rows FW1 to FW5 and SC1 to SC5.

| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FW1 | T | T2 | L0 | the chi parameters of Perrin and Hartmann 1989 for CO2-CO2 and CO2-N2 (`co2_far_wing.json` `chi_co2_co2`, `chi_co2_n2`: sigma1, sigma2, sigma3 of 3, 30, 120 and 3, 10, 70 cm-1, b_i(T) = alpha + beta exp(-epsilon T)) | none | carbon dioxide in carbon dioxide or nitrogen, 190 to 800 K | a measured clear-sky spectrum of a dense carbon dioxide air (Venus near-surface windows) whose wing differs from the factor by more than a factor of 2 | the paper's own tables, or the Tonkov et al. 1996 factor (350 cm-1 truncation) | HELIOS-K files read 10-30; Simonetti and others 2021 (arXiv 2110.11702) equations 12 to 15 read; Wordsworth, Forget and Eymet 2010 compare it with the 25 cm-1 cut |
| FW2 | P | T4 | L0 | the Lorentz half widths of the carbon dioxide lines: 0.10 cm-1 per atmosphere self-broadened, 0.07 foreign (every gas that is not carbon dioxide takes the nitrogen width), exponent 0.7 in (296 K / T) (`co2_far_wing.json` `gamma_self_cm1_atm`, `gamma_foreign_cm1_atm`, `gamma_exponent`) | self 0.09 to 0.11; foreign 0.06 to 0.08; exponent 0.6 to 0.8 | strong lines of the 4.3 and 15 micron bands | the HITRAN widths of the lines that carry the band strength lie outside the knobs | the HITRAN line list (a download, not held) | recalled from HITRAN, not read |
| FW3 | P | T3 | L0 | the reach of the wing, 500 cm-1 (`co2_far_wing.json` `reach_cm1`) | 120 to the whole grid | all carbon dioxide bands | a measured spectrum shows the wing beyond 500 cm-1 or none beyond 120 | a measured truncation | Simonetti and others 2021 use 500 cm-1 for carbon dioxide air and say proposed truncations run from 120 cm-1 to none; the tests sweep the bracket |
| FW4 | P | T3 | L0 | the temperature range of the chi factor, 190 to 800 K, held at the ends (`co2_far_wing.json` `temperature_low_k`, `temperature_high_k`) | 140 to 250 K; 700 to 1000 K | all carbon dioxide air | a chi measured above 800 K that differs from the held value | a chi fit that covers the hot air of a close-in planet | Wordsworth, Forget and Eymet 2010; the form gives a chi above 1 below about 140 K |
| FW5 | P | T5 | L0 | the factor is applied to every carbon dioxide band (it was fitted to the 4.3 micron wing, the file's range is 2100 to 2500 cm-1), the band strength is the k-table mean at 1e-5 bar times the band width, spread evenly over the band, and the line shape inside the cutoff stays the tables' | chi on the 4.3 micron band only | all bands | a line-by-line spectrum that differs from the band-averaged wing by more than a factor of 2 in a window | a line-by-line wing from a carbon dioxide line list | HELIOS-K's own example sets 0 to 10000 cm-1; Bezard and others 2011 quote Meadows and Crisp 1996 for the shape beyond the band |
| SC1 | P | T3 | L1 | the acid cloud base temperature 367 K (`sulfuric_cloud.json` `base_temperature_k`): the first level up the dry adiabat that cool, the ground when it already is | 345 to 367 K; the 38 km level (about 4 bar, temperature not printed) | airs with sulfur dioxide, an oxidiser and water | a Venus-like air whose cloud base lies at another temperature than the dry adiabat gives | a cloud base from the saturation of sulfuric acid in the weight-percent solution | Arney and Kane 2018 (arXiv 1804.05889) Table 1 read: lower cloud 47.5 to 50.5 km, 367 to 345 K, 1.39 to 0.98 bar |
| SC2 | P | T3 | L0 | the acid cloud depth, uniform on 20 to 30 at 550 nm (`optical_depth_low`, `optical_depth_high`), the sum of the published layer depths at 0.63 micron (the wavelength is not corrected) | 6 to 40 | any air that makes the cloud | a second world with an acid cloud whose depth is far outside; a sum that should not add the three layers | a depth from the production rate of the acid | Arney and Kane 2018 Table 1 read: upper cloud 6 to 8, middle 8 to 10, lower 6 to 12 |
| SC3 | P | T3 | L0 | the droplet radius, log-uniform on 0.3 to 3.85 micron, and the density 1800 kg/m3 (`radius_low_um`, `radius_high_um`, `density_kg_m3`): they only set how much acid a depth needs | 0.3 to 4; 1670 to 1840 | any air that makes the cloud | the papers disagree on the mode radii (mode 1 0.30, 0.4 or 0.49; mode 2 1.0, 1.04 or 1.4 micron), so the widest range is used | a size from the cloud microphysics | Gillmann and others 2024 (arXiv 2404.07669) section 3.5.2 and Arney and Kane 2018 Table 2, read; Dai and others 2022 (arXiv 2207.10238) density, a model value |
| SC4 | P | T5 | L1 | the share of the sky under an acid cloud, 1 (`cover`) | 0.5 to 1 | any air that makes the cloud | a second acid-cloud world seen partly clear | a cover from the cloud dynamics | Gillmann and others 2024: the one known world is completely enshrouded |
| SC5 | P | T6 | L1 | the acid supply: the three gates of `acid_medium` (sulfur dioxide, an oxidiser, water), and no production rate, only the limit of all the sulfur dioxide above the base made into acid (`sulfuric_cloud.acid_column_limit_kg_m2`), which binds only in a thin air | none | airs with sulfur dioxide | the limit binds in a thick air in a way the photochemistry would not allow (the acid of a 150 ppm air at 2 bar is hundreds of times the cloud's mass) | a photochemical production rate, with a sink | Gillmann and others 2024 section 3.5.3: cloud-top SO2 5 to 100 ppbv, 130 to 180 ppm below the cloud |

### 6.63 Stand-ins of the acid cloud's infrared optics and the minor-gas wings (GEN.1020)

`physics/cloud_infrared.py` gives the sulfuric acid cloud of `sulfuric_cloud` its infrared opacity on the 130 bands: Mie spheres (`physics/mie.py`) of 76 and 81 percent acid in a gamma size distribution, from the refractive indices of the HITRAN aerosol package (read from the downloaded archive, 10-30), made by `scripts/build_acid_cloud_optics.py` into `acid_cloud_optics.json`. `co2_far_wing.minor_gas_cross_section_cm2` adds the far wing of water, sulfur dioxide and carbon monoxide lines in a carbon dioxide air. In the clear-sky Venus-like test air the cloud takes the sourced-wing result from 539 K to 757 K (a droplet radius of 0.3 micron gives 618 K, 3.85 micron 822 K); the minor-gas wings add under 1 K at their parts-per-million abundances. Report `reports/gen1020-cloud-infrared-2026-10-30.md`. Rows CF1 to CF5, FW6 and FW7.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| CF3 | P | T3 | L1 | `cloud_infrared.TOP_OVER_BASE_PRESSURE` 0.0192: the cloud top pressure over the base pressure | 0.01 to 0.05 | any acid cloud | a cloud whose depth in the air is not spread between these levels | a top from the temperature profile (the tropopause) | Arney and Kane 2018 Table 1 read: upper cloud top 70 km, 0.0267 bar; lower cloud base 47.5 km, 1.391 bar |

### 6.64 Stand-ins of the NH3 active-Sun factor (GEN.794)

`data/photolysis_lifetimes.json` NH3 `activity_factor` (read by `photolysis.rate_per_s` with `active_sun=True`) was the Boss-supplied 1.078 (quiet 1.80e-4 and active 1.94e-4 per s attributed to Huebner and others 1992 and Huebner and Mukherjee 2015, never read). It is now computed by `scripts/nh3_activity_factor.py` from the Leiden NH3 photodissociation cross section and the daily 1 nm solar irradiance of SORCE SOLSTICE, UARS SUSIM and UARS SOLSTICE (120 to 230 nm): each instrument's strongest year over its weakest gives 1.066, 1.083 and 1.060, mean 1.070. No generation code calls the active-Sun factor, so no stored output moves. Report `reports/gen794-nh3-activity-factor-2026-10-30.md`. Rows NA1 and NA2.

| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| NA1 | P | T5 | L0 | the NH3 active-Sun factor of `photolysis_lifetimes.json` 1.07: the rate of the strongest year of a measured solar record over its weakest year, mean of three instruments | 1.05 to 1.15 | the Sun between the cycle minimum and maximum measured since 1991 | an NH3 line of Huebner and Mukherjee 2015 outside 1.05 to 1.15, or a cross section whose 200 to 217 nm part differs from the Leiden one by a different spectral shape | the Huebner and Mukherjee 2015 NH3 quiet and active rates | `scripts/nh3_activity_factor.py` run 10-30 on the Leiden NH3_partial_cross_sections.h5 and the NASA GSFC SORCE SOLSTICE v16, SUSIM v22 and UARS SOLSTICE v18 files; annual medians; the Leiden quiet rate 1.538e-4 per s is reproduced (1.542e-4) |
| NA2 | P | T6 | L0 | the meaning of "active Sun" in the NH3 factor: the strongest measured year of a modern record, not the Huebner and Mukherjee 2015 active Sun, which is stronger than any measured maximum (H2O 1.68 against 1.33 by the earlier lane's method), so the table mixes two conventions (H2O 1.68 and CO2 2.35 on theirs, NH3 on this) | 1.07 to 1.15, the top being NA1 scaled by the H2O ratio of the two conventions | any use of `active_sun=True` for NH3 | an NH3 factor of Huebner and Mukherjee 2015 outside 1.05 to 1.15 | the Huebner and Mukherjee 2015 NH3 quiet and active rates | docs/design/composition/atmosphere-composition-parts/10-verification-second-note-E-nh3-lifetime.md section 3 (H2O 1.33 against 1.68) |

### 6.139 Stand-ins of the carbon and S stars (GEN.1026)

`physics/agb_carbon.py` counts the thermal pulses of a star on the asymptotic branch and the carbon the third dredge-up brings up (design note `docs/design/systems/carbon-stars.md`); the star sheet shows "carbon star" or "S star" as a derived row. The pulse and dredge-up tables are Karakas and Lugaro 2016's; the shape of the dredge-up rise, the core growth and the intershell carbon are stand-ins.

### 6.80 Stand-ins of the 3+1 quadruple stability network (GEN.1029)

`physics/quadruple_stability.py` is the 3+1 classifier of Vynatheya, Mardling and Hamers 2023 (arXiv 2301.09930) as plain numbers: 11 inputs, four hidden layers of 50 logistic units, one output (the probability of "unstable"). The 8,301 numbers are in `quadruple_stability_3p1_weights.json`, made from the authors' scikit-learn model file by `scripts/build_quadruple_stability.py`, which reads the pickle without running its code; the numpy forward pass reproduces scikit-learn to 1e-8. The authors' tests give 93 percent right for 3+1 systems against 66 percent for two nested triples. Nothing calls the module until GEN.1016. Design note `docs/design/systems/quadruple-stability-3p1.md`, report `reports/gen1029-quadruple-stability-2026-10-30.md`. Rows QS1 to QS4.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| QS2 | P | T1 | L0 | the meaning of "stable": bound and not chaotic over 100 outer orbits (a twin with the inner axis larger by 1e-6 au diverging by under 1 percent in the inner axis), `outer_orbits` 100 and `divergence_limit` 0.01 | none | systems that are stable for a stellar lifetime only if nothing slower acts | a system stable at 100 outer orbits that the N-body code breaks at 10^4 outer orbits | a long-integration label | paper section 2, read |

### 6.81 Stand-ins of the 2+2 quadruple stability network (GEN.1031)

`physics/quadruple_stability.py` also holds the 2+2 classifier of Vynatheya, Mardling and Hamers 2023 (arXiv 2301.09930): the same shape as the 3+1 network (11 inputs, four hidden layers of 50 logistic units, one output, 8,301 numbers, `quadruple_stability_2p2_weights.json`), made from the authors' scikit-learn model file by `scripts/build_quadruple_stability.py 2p2`. The authors' tests give 94 percent right for 2+2 systems against 83 percent for two nested triples. Nothing calls it until GEN.1016. Design note `docs/design/systems/quadruple-stability-2p2.md`. ### 6.68 Stand-ins of the open-ocean equilibrium tide (GEN.1034)

`physics/ocean_tides.py` gives the equilibrium tide of each raiser, `(3/2) (m / M) (R / d)^3 R`, which comes out 0.535 m for the Moon on the Earth and 0.246 m for the Sun against the published 0.54 and 0.25 m, summed into the spring and neap ranges, with the floor's `1 + k2 - h2` and the thin-layer loading factor of the sea's weight. It is the open-ocean equilibrium value only: a coast's range depends on the basin and its resonance, which is not modelled. The liquid-dependent rows of the draft (OT3 to OT5, the dominant liquid, the basin depth, the exotic liquids) belong to GEN.1035 and are not built here. Draft `reports/design-drafts/ocean-tides-and-liquids-2026-10-30.md`. The module is not read by the generator until the wiring step (GEN.1034 on train 7).

| OT2 | P | T1 | L0 | ocean_tides.WATER.density_kg_m3 1000 kg/m3, the density of the ocean liquid | 1000 to 1030 | water oceans | a brine ocean whose density moves the loading factor by more than 1 percent (denser than 1060) | the surface liquid table of GEN.1035 (density by liquid and temperature) | NIST density of water at 4 degrees; sea water is 1025, which moves the factor by 0.3 percent |
| OT6 | P | T5 | L1 | ocean_tides.H2_OVER_K2 5/3, the Love number h2 from the interior model's k2 when h2 is not given | 1.5 to 2.2 | homogeneous rocky or icy bodies with a stored k2 | the Earth: measured h2 0.61 and k2 0.30 give 2.0 and a floor share of 0.69; the rule gives 0.80 | the Love numbers h2 from the interior structure (GEN.842 tides) | Love 1909 for a homogeneous incompressible elastic sphere (h2 = 5/3 k2), recalled; checked against the Earth's measured pair |
| OT7 | P | T6 | L0 | ocean_tides.LOCK_TOLERANCE 1e-3, how close to a raiser's orbital rate a body spins to count as locked to it (no oscillating tide from it) | 1e-4 to 1e-2 | bodies the tide chain (GEN.957) has locked | a body whose spin differs from a raiser's rate by 0.5 percent and whose ocean shows a tide of that period | the tide chain's own lock state, read from the stored spin kind | the same share as `moon_history.SYNCHRONOUS_TOLERANCE`; the choice is the project's |

### 6.70 Stand-ins of the ring origin (GEN.1054)

`physics/ring_origin.py` makes a ring the result of the planet's history instead of a chance: the ring of a moon that fell inside the Roche limit (`moon_orbits`, Canup 2010, Hesselbrock and Minton 2017, Black and Mittal 2015, all recalled) and of a comet that passed inside it (Dones 1991, recalled), each spread by the ring-moon cycle, none close to the star (Schlichting and Chang 2011, recalled). A giant with nothing in its past has no ring. The module is not read by the generator until the wiring step (GEN.1057); the draws it replaces, BC20 to BC23 of section 5.2, are re-registered T4, L3 until the ring age and disruption rates of the Cassini end of mission replace them. Saturn (Mimas-sized moon, 1.5e8 years) and Phobos are the tests.

| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| RO2 | P | T4 | L3 | ring_origin.RING_SPREAD_TIME_YR 2e9, the time in which Saturn's rings would lose 1/e of their mass to viscous spreading; the time falls as the ring gets denser and rises with the square of its radius | 5e8 to 1e10 | rings of 1e15 to 1e22 kg | the age of Saturn's rings measured below 1e8 or above 4.5e9 years, or a spreading rate of the main rings over 3 times or under a third of this | the viscous spreading and re-accretion of the ring from its mass, optical depth and particle size (Crida and Charnoz 2012) | the ring-moon cycle of Hesselbrock and Minton 2017 runs over 1e8 to 1e9 years; the age of Saturn's rings is argued between 1e8 and the age of the planet (GEN.1171 replaces the single decay of 5e8 years) |

### 6.90 Stand-ins of the Venus-type spin (GEN.1038)

`physics/venus_spin.py` gives the spin a thick-air rocky planet ends in when the star's thermal tide on its air balances the solid tide: the four equilibria of Correia and Laskar (2001, 2003) as generalised to eccentric orbits and tilt by Cunha, Correia and Laskar (2015, arXiv:1406.4544, read in full), the strength of the thermal tide scaled from Venus, the e-folding time of the tide against the age, and the end state by the planet's birth tilt. It returns Venus's 243 days retrograde (242.9 against the table's 242.9) and no retrograde state for the Earth or Mercury. The design note's 25 percent share (W2, tier T4, tweak L3) is replaced by the tilt rule: the share that ends retrograde is the share of planets born tilted past the split angle, which the census measures once the module is wired. Not read by the generator until GEN.1048 (train 12).

| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| VS1 | D | T1 | L0 | venus_spin.VENUS_STRENGTH 1.9255, the thermal over gravitational tide of Venus in orbital rates (the coefficient of the scaling for every thick-air planet) | none | rocky planets with a thick air | Venus's spin measured to differ from 243.0 days retrograde (the table reproduces -242.9 from this) | the Love number, time lags, scale height and ground flux of each planet (their Table 1 assumes Venus's for all) | Cunha, Correia and Laskar 2015 Table 1, read in full |
| VS2 | D | T1 | L0 | venus_spin.VENUS_DAMPING_TIME_GY 2.3, the e-folding time of Venus's slow-rotation tide | none | rocky planets with a thick air | a spin-down time of Venus computed from its Love number and time lag that differs by a factor of two | the planet's own `k2` and time lag from the interior model | the same Table 1 (tau_eq 2.3 Gyr) |
| VS3 | D | T5 | L0 | venus_spin.strength_over_orbital_rate and damping_time_gy: ws/n goes as L R a^2.5 M^-1.5 / m and tau as m a^6 M^-2 R^-3, from Venus | none | masses 0.1 to 10 Earth, `e` under 0.3 | a 3D climate model of a planet at twice Venus's distance whose thermal torque departs from the law by more than a factor of two (Leconte et al. 2015 find its pressure dependence is not monotonic) | `spin_torques` thermal table (GEN.956) fitted to the same planets | their equations 48, 55 and 68, read; the Venus normalisation is ours |

### 6.91 Stand-ins of the seasonal swing (GEN.1039)

`physics/seasonal_swing.py` gives the temperature through the year at a latitude from the stored tilt and the orbit: the day's mean light through an eccentric orbit, a local energy balance about the annual-mean state with the heat capacity of the sea's mixed layer, the ground's skin and the air column, and the damping by the heat the air carries (the share the means already use). It returns the Earth's tropics within 3 K and its 60 degree zonal mean (ocean share 0.4) at 17 K peak to peak against about 25 K observed, a dry Mars within 8 percent of the fourth-root radiative response, and a polar summer hotter than the equator above a tilt of 54.8 degrees. The first harmonic of a small tilt matches the closed-form response to 0.3 percent. Not read by the generator until GEN.1048 (train 12). Draft: `docs/design/systems/weird-worlds.md` W1.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| SS2 | D | T1 | L0 | seasonal_swing.WATER_HEAT_CAPACITY_J_M3_K 4.1e6, the volumetric heat capacity of liquid water | none | water oceans | a brine or a non-water ocean (ammonia-water, hydrocarbons) | the heat capacity of the surface liquid table (GEN.1035) | water: 4,186 J per kg per K at 1,000 to 1,025 kg per m3 |
| SS3 | D | T5 | L1 | seasonal_swing.MAX_SHARE 0.999, the transport share is cut here; the seasonal damping is the mean state's own, `B_t = B s / (1 - s)` | 0.99 to 0.9999 | air columns up to Venus's | a general circulation model of a thick-air planet whose seasonal swing is not damped like its pole-to-equator contrast | a diffusion coefficient for the seasonal anomalies from the circulation (the seasonal cycle of a 90 bar air) | extends `regional_temperature.TRANSPORT_X0` (DESIGN) from the means to the cycle; the energy balance form is that of North and Coakley 1979, recalled |

### 6.92 Stand-ins of the resonant chains (GEN.1043)

`physics/resonant_chains.py` finds the chains of three or more planets whose neighbouring periods are near 2:1, 3:2, 4:3 or 5:3, and gives the eccentricity each neighbour forces on a planet near a first-order resonance (`e_inner = mu' alpha |f_27| / ((p - 1) |Delta|)`, `e_outer = mu |f_31| / (p |Delta|)`, the Laplace-coefficient resonant terms of the disturbing function, Murray and Dermott 1999 and Lithwick, Xie and Wu 2012, recalled and derived), held at the width of the resonance and short of crossing orbits. It finds TRAPPIST-1 as one chain of seven, Kepler-223 as a chain of four, the Galilean moons as a chain of three and the Solar System as none; Io comes out at 0.0052 against the observed forced 0.0041 and Europa at 0.0083 against 0.0094; TRAPPIST-1's d to h come out between 0.003 and 0.007, the design note's thousandths. Not read by the generator until GEN.1049.
