### 6.140 Stand-ins of the gas disc of a satellite galaxy (GEN.1192)

A satellite's gas is an exponential disc longer than its stars, forming stars by the Schmidt-Kennicutt law that steepens where the gas thins (`galaxy/satellite_history.py`; the same law as the main disc's, `galaxy/star_formation.py`). The present rate comes from the satellite's history, the central gas density is what makes the disc's integrated rate that, and the gas at the half-mass radius is tested against the star formation threshold. Checked against the Large Cloud's HI (4.8e8 solar masses, Staveley-Smith et al. 2003, abstract read: generated 1.05e9, a factor 2.2 high) and the Small Cloud's (about 4e8 recalled: generated 1.95e8, a factor 2 low).

| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SG1 | P | T6 | L1 | tuning.SATELLITE_GAS_TO_STELLAR_SCALE_LENGTH_RATIO 1.8: the scale length of a satellite's gas disc over its stars' | 1.0 to 2.6 (Swaters et al. 2002: 1.8 +/- 0.8) | satellite galaxies that form stars, 3e8 to 2e9 solar masses of stars | a measured HI scale length of a Cloud-sized dwarf outside 1.0 to 2.6 times its stellar scale length, or a generated Cloud gas mass off the observed by more than a factor of 3 | the HI and stellar scale lengths of the Clouds and the dwarfs of LITTLE THINGS or the Westerbork survey | Swaters, van Albada, van der Hulst and Sancisi 2002 (A&A 390, 829, arXiv astro-ph/0204525, abstract READ): the ratio of HI extent to optical diameter, the extent 6.4 disc scale lengths, is 1.8 +/- 0.8; the extent ratio is read as the scale length ratio (an inference). Large Cloud gas test_satellite_history::test_the_large_cloud_forms_stars_at_about_the_rate_and_with_about_the_gas_observed |

### 6.141 Stand-ins of the gas of a satellite galaxy by its distance from the host (GEN.1193)

A satellite within the stripping radius of its host has lost its gas unless it is heavy; beyond it most keep theirs (`generation/satellites.py`, link 7). The effect is on the mean stellar age alone: a stripped satellite's age is raised to just past the oldest at which `galaxy.satellite_history` still forms stars, a gas-keeping one's lowered to that age, so only the satellites near the star formation threshold move (about 5 of 640 in eight Milky Way mass halos, a few in 1e7 to 4e8 solar masses). The light end cannot form stars at any age under the 3 solar masses per pc2 threshold (`SATELLITE_STAR_FORMATION_THRESHOLD_MSUN_PC2`), so the gas-bearing dwarfs of 1e5 to 1e7 solar masses that the survey finds beyond 270 kpc (Leo T) are not made: a separate gap, not closed here.

| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ST1 | P | T6 | L1 | satellites.STRIPPING_RADIUS_MILKY_WAY_KPC 270, scaled with the host's virial radius | 250 to 280 kpc for the Milky Way (Grcevich and Putman 2009) | dwarfs of a host of any halo mass; the scaling with the virial radius is an inference | a survey of another host's dwarfs with the HI cutoff off the scaled radius by more than a factor of 2 | the ram-pressure stripping radius of the host's halo gas and a satellite's orbit (GEN.339) | Grcevich and Putman 2009 (ApJ 696, 385, arXiv:0901.4975, abstract and section 3.4 READ): the majority of dwarfs within about 270 kpc are undetected in HI (under 1e5 solar masses), those beyond predominantly detected, the cutoff between 250 and 280 kpc; ram pressure the likely cause. test_satellite_law::test_the_stripping_radius_is_270_kpc_for_the_milky_ways_halo_and_scales_with_the_virial_radius |
| ST2 | P | T6 | L1 | satellites.STRIPPING_RETAINED_MIN_STELLAR_MASS_SOLAR 4.0e8: the lightest satellite that keeps its gas inside the stripping radius | 2e8 to 4.6e8 | satellites inside the stripping radius | a gas-rich satellite lighter than 2e8 inside 270 kpc of its host (or a Cloud-mass one found stripped there) | the ram pressure against the anchoring pressure of the satellite's gas and stars on its orbit | the Small Magellanic Cloud (4.6e8 solar masses of stars, 64 kpc, HI about 4e8 recalled) keeps its gas; Grcevich and Putman 2009 footnote 3 (READ) leave both Clouds out for their large mass. test_satellite_law::test_a_satellite_as_heavy_as_the_small_cloud_keeps_its_gas_inside_the_stripping_radius |
| ST3 | B | T1 | L0 | satellites.GAS_DETECTED_BEYOND_STRIPPING 0.875: the share of satellites beyond the stripping radius that hold gas | 0.7 to 0.95 (14 of 16, small number) | dwarfs beyond 280 kpc | a larger sample beyond 280 kpc with a detection share outside 0.7 to 0.95 | a larger survey of far dwarfs (the HI mass of Tucana and Cetus, the two non-detections, is under the limit) | Grcevich and Putman 2009 (section 3.4 READ): beyond 280 kpc, 14 galaxies detected at over 1e5 solar masses and two (Tucana and Cetus) not. test_satellite_law::test_a_satellite_beyond_the_stripping_radius_keeps_its_gas_for_the_share_that_does |

### 6.134 Stand-ins of the structure of a giant (GEN.733)

The pressure and radius at every mass inside a gas giant, an ice giant or a brown dwarf (`physics/giant_structure.py`, the density of the hydrogen and helium from the download of fill GS1, `datasets/hhe_eos.py`), which `giant_interior.py` reads the pressure at each layer boundary from. Kinds, tiers and tweak levels as in 6.1 and in the list of every stand-in. The radius is the generator's (GEN.460) and the model takes the adiabat that closes on it (`giant_structure.fit_adiabat`), so what the structure says of the temperature is a result and not an input. Not in it: a dilute core or a helium gradient as a structure (the layers of `giant_interior.py` are placed on the profile, the profile does not see them), the temperature of an irradiated envelope (an inflated giant is on the adiabat whose one-bar temperature is the model's), rotation and its shape, and the heat of formation.

| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | evidence | constant | knobs | validity | replaced_by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GS2 | T | T5 | L0 | the heavy elements sit in a core and a mixed envelope of the envelope metallicity Z of GEN.460, with a core of what is left (no dilute core, no gradient, no helium rain inside the structure); giant-interiors.md section 2 (Helled and Stevenson 2024; Mankovich and Fortney 2020) say a real giant is more mixed, which moves the central pressure more than the boundary pressures | giant_structure.StructureSubject.resolved | the layers of the subject | any gas giant, ice giant or brown dwarf | a structure with the dilute core and the gradient the layers of `giant_interior.py` carry |
| GS3 | T | T5 | L1 | hydrogen and helium mix by the ideal volume law, the specific volume of the Y = 0.275 mixture of the table moved by the volume difference of the two pure tables over the mixture's (`ivl_slope`), the way Chabrier et al. 2019 mix them; the table's own interaction term is kept at 0.275 and the slope is held to the range a physical fluid can have; the helium share is the natal one for the whole body (rain is not in the structure) | hhe_eos.IVL_SLOPE_RANGE | -1.2 to 3.0, fixed | helium shares near 0.275 (0.2 to 0.35) | the tables of Chabrier and Debras 2021 at other Y, or a helium-rain structure |
| GS4 | T | T5 | L0 | the density of the heavy elements is a volume sum (the ideal volume law) of the repository's iron (Vinet), silicate and water curves, each at its own fixed temperature, tabulated on 220 nodes in ln P from 1 bar to 1e17 Pa; ammonia and methane ice take the water curve | giant_structure.HEAVY_NODES_PA | 1e5 to 1e17 Pa, 220 nodes | the core, the mantle and the heavy elements mixed into the envelope | equations of state of ammonia and methane and of mixtures at the temperature of the adiabat |
| GS5 | T | T5 | L0 | the tables start at 100 K, so an adiabat colder than that is carried up the molecular gas's own adiabat, d ln T / d ln P of a diatomic ideal gas (2/7), to where it reaches 100 K and the entropy there is read; the one-bar temperature the model reports is the inverse of that, good to a few percent | hhe_eos.DIATOMIC_ADIABATIC_GRADIENT | 2/7, fixed | the one-bar temperature of an ice giant or a cold gas giant (under 100 K) | a hydrogen-helium table to 30 K, or a molecular-hydrogen equation of state with the ortho-para states |
| GS6 | D | T3 | L0 | the values of the tables in the solid phase, beyond the first-order quantum correction of the ions and at very low density are unphysical (the archive's README); they are read as they stand and the volume slope is bounded (GS3), and the adiabat is a line of the table's entropy because the table's adiabatic gradient carries numerical noise; the fit stops where the radius is met to 2e-4 | giant_structure.RADIUS_TOLERANCE | 2e-4 of the radius, fixed | a body whose adiabat stays out of the solid and low-density regions (all giants) | the tables' own newer release |
| GS7 | T | T6 | L0 | the integration stops where 0.2 percent of the mass is left inside (or at 2 percent of the radius) and closes the rest as a sphere of the density there; the central pressure is the sphere's, which is within the step of the model's accuracy for the boundary pressures and good to some tens of percent for the centre of a body with a dense core | giant_structure.CENTRE_MASS_FRACTION, CENTRE_RADIUS_FRACTION | 2e-3 and 0.02, fixed | the centre of every giant | an integration to the centre with the core's own equation of state |

### 6.135 Stand-ins of a giant's internal heat (GEN.810)

The heat a gas giant, ice giant or brown dwarf radiates of its own (`physics/giant_heat.py`), stored as its `internal_heat_flux_w_m2`: a base from the cooling law of GEN.816 at its mass, radius and age (its stand-ins are that item's) and, for a giant whose helium rained out of the molecular envelope, the energy the sinking helium gave up (the layers' radii and helium, `helium_depletion` and `has_helium_shell` are what it reads). Kinds, tiers and tweak levels as in 6.1 and in the list of every stand-in. Not in it: the heat of a hot giant's inflation, the energy neon or heavy elements give up when they sink, and a feedback of the rain's heat on the one-bar temperature that decided the rain.

| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | evidence | constant | knobs | validity | replaced_by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GH2 | T | T5 | L0 | half of the gravitational energy the sinking helium gives up leaves as heat and half warms the interior it falls through (the virial split); the energy is the mass that sank times the potential it fell through, from the mean of the layers that lost helium to the mean of those that gained it, each layer a shell of uniform density | giant_heat.RAIN_HEAT_SHARE | 0.5, 0.3 to 1 | a giant with a helium shell, a gradient or a dilute core | an evolution model with the helium separation (Mankovich and Fortney 2020) |
| GH3 | P | T4 | L0 | the heat has come out over the star's whole age, an average that is a lower bound for the power today since the rain began when the body cooled below the separation temperature | giant_heat.RAIN_AGE_SHARE | 1.0, 0.3 to 1 | any giant | the time since the one-bar temperature crossed the onset (needs the cooling track) |
| GH4 | T | T6 | L0 | approximations: the helium that moved goes from every layer that lost it to every layer that gained it at their means; the potential is at the half-mass radius of each layer; one pass, no feedback of the rain's heat on the one-bar temperature that decided the rain (the draws keep their order) | giant_heat.rain_energy_j | none | every giant | a coupled evolution of the structure and the helium |

### 6.136 Stand-ins of the core's thermal history (GEN.838)

Rows for the core model beside the mantle box (`physics/core_heat.py`). Thiriet et al. 2019 was not readable, so the boundary-layer law and the mantle box's table are taken second-hand (`reports/secondhand-citations-2026-10-24.md`); the iron liquidus, the core adiabat and the Earth's core pressures are the usual textbook anchors (Alfe 2002, Labrosse 2003, Lay et al. 2008, all second-hand). Tiers and tweaks are the lane's reading (Core 4), for the owner to confirm.

<!-- core-heat-standins:begin -->
| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CC1 | P | T6 | L2 | core_heat.PRESSURE_FACTOR 1.16 (the compression a uniform shell leaves out) | 1.0 to 1.3 | rocky bodies up to a few Earth masses | a layered structure's pressure at the core-mantle boundary and centre of a super-Earth | the pressures of `bulk_structure.layered_structure` | tuned to the Earth's 136 GPa at the boundary (centre 337 against 364 GPa) |
| CC2 | P | T6 | L1 | core_heat.MANTLE_ADIABAT_K_GPA 8 K per GPa from the box's temperature to the base of the mantle | 5 to 10 | rocky bodies | a mantle adiabat from a heavier body's equation of state | an adiabat from the mantle's density and expansivity | design (the Earth's potential 1,600 K to 2,700 K at the boundary; the box's temperature is the mantle's mean) |
| CC3 | P | T5 | L2 | core_heat.CORE_ADIABAT_K_GPA 6.74 K per GPa below the top of the core | 5 to 8 | all cores | a core adiabat from a Gruneisen parameter at a super-Earth's pressures | a Gruneisen adiabat | tuned to the Earth's 4,100 K at 136 GPa and 5,400 K at 329 GPa (Alfe 2002, second-hand) |
| CC4 | P | T3 | L2 | core_heat.LIQUIDUS_ZERO_K 1811 and LIQUIDUS_ANCHOR (329 GPa, 5,400 K): the liquidus `1811 sqrt(1 + P / A)` of pure iron | the anchor 5,000 to 6,200 K | cores under about 400 GPa | a liquidus of an iron alloy with the light elements and the sulfur of Mercury's core | an alloy liquidus | the Earth's inner core boundary (Alfe 2002, second-hand); no light elements, so a Mercury-like core freezes too early |
| CC5 | P | T4 | L0 | core_heat.CORE_CONDUCTIVITY 40 W/(m K) | 30 to 100 | all cores | a conductivity read for the core's alloy at its pressure | a measured conductivity | the literature spans 30 to 100 (Pozzo 2012 and Gomi 2013, second-hand) |
| CC6 | P | T5 | L1 | core_heat.LATENT_HEAT_J_KG 2.5e5 and GRAVITATIONAL_ENERGY_J_KG 2.5e5 per kg of inner core that freezes | each 1e5 to 6e5 | cores with an inner core | an energy budget of the Earth's inner core growth | a composition-dependent budget | Labrosse 2003 (second-hand): the latent heat and the light elements' energy are of one order |
| CC7 | P | T7 | L3 | core_heat.ACTIVATION_VOLUME_M3_MOL 4.7e-6 over PRESSURE_SCALE_GPA 100: the activation enthalpy of the lowest mantle layer gains `V* P0 ln(1 + P / P0)` | V* 3 to 6 cm3/mol, P0 50 to 200 GPa | rocky bodies | a viscosity of the lowest mantle from a pressure-dependent law | a calibrated lower-mantle viscosity | hand-tuned so the Earth's core ends at 4,080 K with an inner core of 0.4 of its radius and 5.7 TW across the boundary (5 to 15 TW, Lay et al. 2008, second-hand) |
| CC8 | P | T7 | L0 | core_heat.INITIAL_EXCESS_K 1,000 K the core starts above the base of the mantle | 500 to 1,500 | all cores | a core formation model with the energy of the sinking iron | a differentiation model | design (the sinking iron's energy goes into the core; Stevenson 1983, second-hand) |
| CC9 | P | T9 | L3 | core_heat.MIN_LIQUID_SHELL 0.25: a liquid shell thinner than this share of the core's radius runs no dynamo | 0.15 to 0.35 | all cores | a thin-shell dynamo model | a dynamo criterion from the shell's geometry | needed output: Mars (shell 0.23) has no field at 4.5 Gy and the Earth (0.6) has one |
| CC10 | P | T7 | L2 | core_heat.EARTH_CORE_RADIUS_M, EARTH_CORE_DENSITY_KG_M3 and EARTH_DYNAMO_W: the unit of the dipole moment's buoyancy-flux scaling `(R / R_e)^2 (P / P_e)^(1/3) (rho / rho_e)^(1/6)` | the exponents | cores with a running dynamo | a measured field of a body with another core | the full scaling of Christensen 2010 | the Earth's moment 7.8e22 A m2 (the scaling is read second-hand; the unit is the model's own Earth) |
| CC11 | P | T6 | L0 | The mantle box is not changed by the core (the core's heat is already its heat capacity); the core is driven by the box's temperature history | none | all bodies | a two-way coupled model whose mantle cools differently | a coupled core and mantle box | design (the stored heat flow and Urey ratio stay as they were) |

What `magnetism.generate_field` does with the dynamo module (section 6.30) and the core model (section 6.59): every rocky body, moon and giant takes its moment from `dynamo.field`, and the three design defaults it replaces (the 4 Gyr lifetime and 7.8e22 A m2 times mass, the three giant anchors, the Ganymede-like chance) are gone.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| DY6 | P | T9 | L2 | magnetism.CORE_POWER_SCALE 0.479: the core model's Earth drives its dynamo with 2.4 TW (CC10) and the scaling's Earth with 1.15 TW (DY3), so the model's power is multiplied by the ratio to put the model's Earth on the scaling's (moment 0.84 of Earth's, dipole share 0.65) | 0.3 to 1 | rocky and icy cores of the thermal model | an Earth-mass planet of the model whose moment is outside 0.5 to 2 times Earth's | one Earth calibration for both the thermal model and the scaling | tuned so the model's Earth reproduces the scaling's Earth |
| DY7 | P | T9 | L3 | magnetism.MERCURY_MOMENT_A_M2 3e19, MERCURY_SCATTER_DEX 0.5: a body of 0.05 to 0.1 Earth masses whose bulk density says its core fraction is over 0.4 keeps this moment whatever the core model says, because its iron-sulfide core melts at lower temperatures than the pure iron the model has | moment 1e19 to 1e20 | small iron-rich planets | a small iron-rich body whose core model says dead and whose field is alive | a core model with a sulfur-bearing liquidus | the design default of GEN.86, a Solar System value (Mercury) kept until the sulfur liquidus replaces it |

### 6.143 Stand-ins of the belt dose a moon takes (GEN.983)

What `radiation_belts.moon_exposure_msv_yr` and `radiation.apply_parent_belts` add to the belts of section 6.15: the dose a moon takes at its orbit inside its parent's belts, stored as `dose_belt_msv_yr` and part of `surface_dose_msv_yr`. The belts themselves (their edges, peaks and intensity) are the rows of 6.15 and are now read by the generator. Not in it: the moon's own field and the minimagnetosphere it makes in its parent's, the electrons and protons below the aluminium shield's cut (the dose of a bare ground is higher than the dose behind 100 mil), the particles a moon sweeps out of the belt, an eccentric orbit (the semi-major axis is used), a rocky planet's own belts (they lie above its air).

| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| RB9 | T | T3 | L0 | radiation_belts.BELT_WEIGHT: the effective dose per absorbed dose of the belts' particles, 1 Sv per Gy for electrons and 2 for protons (ICRP 103's radiation weighting factors; second-hand, the standard cannot be read) | protons 2 to 5 | the belts of a dipole | a dose measurement of the belts' mixed field in Sv | a belt model with a spectrum and a fluence-to-dose table | ICRP 103 (second-hand) |
| RB10 | P | T7 | L1 | radiation_belts.SURFACE_VIEW_FRACTION 0.5: the ground takes the belt dose from the half of the sky the body does not block | 0.35 to 0.6 | an airless or thin-aired moon | a dose map of a moon in a belt against the open-orbit rate | an angular flux through the body's shadow and the field's pitch-angle distribution | geometry, the dose behind the standard shield taken as the dose in open space |
| RB11 | P | T7 | L1 | radiation_belts.STOP_COLUMN_G_CM2 2.0: the air column that cuts the belts' dose by a factor of e (MeV electrons go about 1 g/cm2, tens of MeV protons 1 to 3) | 1 to 4 | a moon with air | a dose through a thick air or a depth-dose curve of the belt spectrum that is not e-folding | a depth-dose curve of the belt spectrum through air (the PSTAR and ESTAR ranges the proton cutoff already reads, `proton_range`) | ranges of electrons and protons recalled from NIST |

### 6.144 Stand-ins of the induced field of cores and of a planet in its star's field (GEN.981)

What `magnetism.sphere_response`, `core_induced_moment` and `generate_stellar_induction` add to the induced field of an ocean moon (GEN.178): the answer of a metal core to a changing field (a moon's in its parent's, a planet's in its star's) and of a planet's ocean. The sphere's response is the exact one of a homogeneous conducting sphere (no stand-in). Not in it: a conducting mantle (the lower mantle's 1 to 10 S/m would add to the core's and the ocean's), the unevenness of land and ocean, the planet's induced magnetosphere of an ionosphere (Venus), a stellar field that changes in time faster than the planet turns (flares, sector crossings), and a giant's metallic layer.

| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| IN1 | P | T3 | L0 | magnetism.CORE_CONDUCTIVITY_S_M 1.0e6: the electrical conductivity of an iron-alloy core, S/m, the same for a solid one (Earth's outer core at its pressure and temperature, 1.0 to 1.5 million; second-hand) | 0.5e6 to 1.5e6 | metal cores | a core whose induced answer differs (the response is 1 for any core many skin depths across, so the knob matters only for a small or a slowly excited core) | a conductivity from the core's own alloy and temperature (the core model's light elements) | Pozzo et al. 2012 and Gomi et al. 2013 (second-hand) |
| IN2 | P | T3 | L0 | magnetism.IMF_EARTH_T 5e-9: the field a star's wind carries at 1 AU, T (the Sun's interplanetary field, Parker spiral), scaled to another star and distance by the square root of the wind's pressure over Earth's (the field's energy a fixed share of the wind's) | 3e-9 to 8e-9 | a star with a wind of the activity model (`wind_pressure_pa`) | a measured interplanetary field of another star (none) | a field from the star's own surface field and the wind's speed | recalled (Parker 1958; the Sun's field at 1 AU is 3 to 6 nT) |
| IN3 | P | T7 | L1 | magnetism.STELLAR_EXCITATION_SHARE 0.5: the share of the wind's field that a planet sees as a changing field (the part perpendicular to its spin axis, averaged over the tilt) | 0.3 to 0.8 | a rocky planet | a measured induced response of a planet to the interplanetary field | the planet's axial tilt and the field's direction (the orientation draws) | geometry: the mean sine of an angle on a sphere is 0.785, the spiral takes it lower |
| IN4 | P | T7 | L1 | the share of a surface ocean's covering that counts as a shell (the conductance times the ocean fraction) and the field a moon's or planet's core takes past an ocean (the square root of one less the ocean's answer squared), both in `magnetism._ocean_and_core_moment` | none | a body with an ocean and a metal core | an induction sounding of a body with both | a layered induction model with the mantle | design: a one-layer approximation of a layered Earth-like response |

### 6.155 Stand-ins of the life readings (GEN.1210)

The words `physics/life_readings.py` says a world's life in: how much of each kind (abundance), which way it is heading (trend) and how long its conditions last (security). Every number is a design number read from stored values only; nothing is stored and nothing generated reads them.

| LR2 | P | T6 | L3 | life_readings.TREND_SPAN_GYR 0.5, TREND_BAND 0.10 and SPREAD_GYR 0.3: the conditions are read half a billion years ahead, a gain or loss of a tenth of the class likelihood is a trend, and a kind is still spreading for 0.3 Gyr after its milestone | span 0.25 to 1; band 0.05 to 0.2; spread 0.1 to 1 | a world with a living class and a main-sequence star | none known | the spread of a kind over its world after the milestone, once the stage model carries one | hand-picked against the brightening of the Sun, about 7 percent a Gyr in `main_sequence`; no source gives how long a kind takes to fill its world |
| LR3 | P | T6 | L3 | life_readings.DYING_GYR 0.1, PRECARIOUS_GYR 0.5, MARGINAL_GYR 2.0 and MARGINAL_SCORE 2: the time left, Gyr, within which a class is Dying out, Precarious and Marginal, and the score at or under which it is Marginal now | each time a factor of 2; score 1 to 3 | a world with a living class | a habitability record that says how long a biosphere survives the loss of its conditions | the lifetimes of biospheres under a brightening star (Caldeira and Kasting 1992 give about 1 Gyr for Earth's, not opened) | hand-picked; the step of the search is 0.1 Gyr |
| LR4 | P | T5 | L0 | life_readings.TEMPERATURE_EXPONENT 0.25: a surface warms as the fourth root of the star's luminosity, with the greenhouse, the albedo and the ice held where they are, and an open sea is lost where the light passes the runaway-greenhouse limit (`orbits.runaway_greenhouse_flux`, Kopparapu et al. 2014) | none | a world that radiates what it absorbs | a feedback that moves the surface by more than the bare fourth root over half a billion years | the climate solve run at the later luminosity | the Stefan-Boltzmann law |
| LR5 | P | T6 | L3 | life_readings.KIND_MILESTONES: microbes spread after abiogenesis, flora and fauna after multicellularity, sentient life after the technological civilization | none | a world with a stored schedule | a kind that appears at another step of the schedule | the kind scores carrying their own ages | reasoned from `physics/life_complexity.py`: the rung at which each kind becomes possible |

### 6.157 Stand-ins of the galactic potential (GEN.338)

`galaxy/potential.py` is the galaxy's pull and potential from three kinds of mass: Hernquist spheres, Miyamoto-Nagai disks and an NFW halo, with a softened black hole. Every mass is the generated galaxy's (the density model's components and the mass chain's halo); only the matching of the density model's shapes to those three analytic profiles is a stand-in. Not read by the generator until the stepper and the acceleration kernel (GEN.340, GEN.342).

| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GP1 | P | T6 | L2 | potential.DISK_COMPONENTS: the thin disk, the thick disk and the nuclear disc are each one Miyamoto-Nagai disk of the component's mass with `a` its scale length and `b` its scale height | `a` 1 to 1.5 times the scale length, `b` 1 to 2 times the scale height | a galaxy whose disks are exponential in radius and height | a numerical potential of the exponential disk that differs from the Miyamoto-Nagai one by over 10 percent in the circular speed between 0.3 and 4 scale lengths | the potential of the exponential disk solved numerically (Poisson solver or the Bessel-function form of Freeman 1970) | Miyamoto and Nagai 1975 for the profile; the pairing with the exponential disk is the habit of Bovy 2015 (MWPotential2014 fits `a` = 3 kpc, `b` = 280 pc to a disk of 2.6 kpc scale length), recalled |
| GP2 | P | T6 | L2 | potential.HERNQUIST_COMPONENTS: the bulge (with its bar's long axis as the scale radius), the stellar halo (its break radius) and the nuclear cluster (its Hernquist scale) are each one Hernquist sphere of the component's mass | the bulge's scale 0.5 to 2 times the density model's long axis | a bar or spheroid whose mass within a few scale radii is spherical enough | a bar's own potential (the three axes) that differs from a sphere's in the plane by over 10 percent between 0.3 and 3 scale lengths | the triaxial bar potential of the density model (a Poisson solve on the grid of `enclosed_mass`) | Hernquist 1990 for the profile; the bulge density model's long axis as scale is this lane's choice |
| GP3 | P | T4 | L0 | potential.BLACK_HOLE_SOFTENING_PC 1.0: the central black hole is a Plummer point of 1 pc softening, the only softened part of the potential | 0.1 to 3 pc | a hole of 1e5 to 1e10 solar masses | an orbit within 1 pc of the hole whose pericentre matters | the post-Newtonian orbit of a star near the hole | Boss's rule in GEN.338: Plummer softening of 1 pc for the central black hole only |

### 6.163 Stand-ins of the helium-core white dwarf (GEN.996)

`physics/helium_core_white_dwarf.py` gives the white dwarf a giant of under 2.3 solar masses leaves when it fills its Roche lobe on the red giant branch: its helium core at that moment, 0.18 to 0.45 solar masses. The share of white dwarfs that are helium-core is not set: it follows from the generator's own period law and mass-ratio law (about 6 percent for 1 to 1.5 solar mass progenitors against 7.4 percent of the 20 pc sample). Two places read it. The tree (`multistar._Builder.helium_core`) gives a companion white dwarf in a post-common-envelope pair the core its pair's axis says, and the star draw (`helium_core_draw`, called by `Star`) makes a drawn white dwarf a helium core with the share the same pair laws leave, after which the tree forces the matching companion on the axis the core says (`multistar._bind_helium_core_companion`). The stored-member rebuild (`stored_trees.helium_core_mass_sol`, `member_record.make`) needs no flag: a white dwarf lighter than the flash core is a helium core. Tiers T1 to T9 and tweak levels L0 to L4 as in 6.6a (GEN.725); the lane's reading (Bug 1), for the owner to confirm.

<!-- helium-core-wd-standins:begin -->
| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| HC1 | P | T1 | L0 | tuning.HELIUM_FLASH_CORE_MASS_SOL 0.45 and HELIUM_CORE_PROGENITOR_MAX_SOL 2.3: the core mass at the helium flash and the heaviest star with a degenerate core | 0.45 to 0.47; 2.0 to 2.3 | stars of 0.8 to 2.3 solar masses | a helium flash at a core mass outside 0.43 to 0.5 in a published track set | a track set read for the core mass at the tip by mass and metallicity | READ (Tauris and van den Heuvel 2006, section 16.2 of the review, astro-ph/0303456: "about 0.45 Msun at helium flash ignition" for all stars under 2.3 Msun) |
| HC2 | P | T2 | L0 | tuning.GIANT_RADIUS_BY_CORE_MASS_RSUN: R0 5500 at Z 0.02 and 3300 at Z 0.001 of Rappaport et al. 1995's R = R0 Mc^4.5 / (1 + 4 Mc^4) + 0.5 solar radii; straight in log Z between, held beyond | R0 3300 to 5500 | red giants of 0.8 to 2 solar masses with a core of 0.18 to 0.45 | a red giant track whose radius at a 0.3 solar mass core differs from 24 solar radii (Pop I) by more than 30 percent | a detailed binary track set (MESA) for the core at overflow, since the relation is not accurate at small cores (Tauris and Savonije 1999) | READ SECOND HAND (the formula and the two R0 as printed in Li et al. 2024, arXiv 2410.14151, and Chen et al. 2013, arXiv 1203.3439; Rappaport et al. 1995 itself is not opened); the log Z line between the two points is an inference |
| HC3 | P | T2 | L1 | tuning.HELIUM_CORE_MIN_MASS_SOL 0.18: under it a giant that fills its lobe is a subgiant (contact or Algol), not a helium white dwarf maker | 0.15 to 0.2 | pairs whose lobe is 4 to 6 solar radii | a helium white dwarf of mass under 0.18 made on the red giant branch by stable overflow | the lower end of a detailed binary track set | READ SECOND HAND (Tauris and Savonije 1999 fit the white dwarf mass and orbital period for 0.18 to 0.45, as quoted by Chen et al. 2013); the cut is read as the edge of the relation |
| HC4 | P | T7 | L0 | helium_core_white_dwarf.overflow_core_mass_sol: the giant fills its lobe at the pair's axis on a circle (the production calls pass e = 0, the orbit a stripped giant's mass transfer circularises) with an Eggleton lobe; the core at that moment is the white dwarf, whether the overflow is stable or a common envelope that is ejected; the companion's mass is its birth mass when it evolves after the giant (it is on the main sequence then) and its present mass when it is a remnant already | none | pairs of a giant with a companion of any mass that survives | a common envelope that leaves a core different from the one at the onset by more than 10 percent (the core grows during an unstable overflow) | a common-envelope model with the energy budget | an inference (Tauris and van den Heuvel 2006: a helium core in a binary keeps the mass it had at detachment); the merger of a pair too unequal to eject the envelope is not modelled |
| HC5 | P | T7 | L0 | the cooling age of a helium-core white dwarf is the one of every white dwarf of its progenitor (the age less the end of the progenitor's whole life, `stellar_evolution.cooling_age_gy`), though the envelope left earlier on the red giant branch | none | the cooling age differs by the horizontal-branch and asymptotic-branch time (a few percent of the age of a solar-mass star's white dwarf) | a binary track set that gives the cooling age from the overflow | a detailed binary track set (MESA) for the age at detachment | an inference (the white dwarf cools from the moment its envelope is gone; the lane leaves out the time between overflow and the end of the progenitor's life) |
| HC6 | P | T7 | L0 | helium_core_draw.draw_core_mass_sol: a drawn white dwarf is a helium core when the pair it would have had is in the window: the chance of a companion from the `star-count` table (with the [Fe/H] trend of the close-companion chance), the companion's birth mass from the tree's mass-ratio law and its period from the tree's own period law (tight-tail weight included), the axis floor the two zero-age radii touching (R of M^0.8 solar radii, `ZERO_AGE_RADIUS_EXPONENT`); no rate is set | none | the share of white dwarfs of 1 to 1.5 solar mass progenitors that are helium cores: 6 to 9 percent (the 20 pc sample has 7.4 percent under 0.45 solar masses, Giammichele et al. 2012) | a white dwarf census of a volume-limited sample whose helium-core share is outside 3 to 15 percent | the period and mass-ratio laws of GEN.951 when they are the tree's, and a binary population synthesis | an inference (the laws are the generator's; the share is what they leave in the window) |
| HC7 | P | T7 | L1 | multistar._bind_helium_core_companion: the companion of a helium-core white dwarf that the star draw made is on the circle at which its giant filled its lobe with that core (`overflow_axis_au`), is a star of the tree's mass law, and the first star of the roster; the pair is labelled post-common-envelope; the white dwarf's largest radius is the giant's at the overflow | none | systems of a helium-core white dwarf and a star | an observed helium white dwarf with a main-sequence companion whose period is outside the Rappaport relation by more than a factor of 2 | Tauris and Savonije 1999's period against core mass relation, when read | an inference (the relation between the core and the final period is the lobe-filling one; the companion's mass is drawn again, not the one the star draw used, since only the core is stored) |
<!-- helium-core-wd-standins:end -->

### 6.159 Stand-ins of the star stepper (GEN.340)

`galaxy/stepper.py` steps stars through the galactic potential with kick-drift-kick, each by its own block step. The numbers are the item's own; the timescale and the step cap are choices of the module.

| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ST1 | P | T6 | L0 | stepper.STEP_ETA 0.05 and STEP_CAP_MYR 5: a step is a twentieth of the star's own crossing time (the smaller of r/v and sqrt(r/a) at its present point, the cheap stand-in for the pericentre crossing time), no more than 5 Myr | eta 0.02 to 0.1; cap 1 to 10 Myr | a star in the smooth potential with no close encounter | an orbit of high eccentricity whose pericentre passage is poorly resolved at the present-point timescale (the energy test fails past 1e-3 per Gyr) | the pericentre crossing time from the orbit's energy and angular momentum | GEN.340's own wording ("step eta 0.05 of the pericentre crossing time, capped at 5 Myr"); kick-drift-kick is second-order symplectic (Hairer, Lubich and Wanner 2006, recalled) |
| ST2 | P | T4 | L0 | stepper.MAX_LEVEL 12: a star needs at most 4096 equal steps in one span; past it the star is stepped at that cap and flagged | 8 to 16 | any span the orbital update asks for | a span so long that the flagged cap leaves the orbit's energy off by over 1e-3 | no cap, once the update's span is bounded by the next-update rule (GEN.105) | GEN.340's own wording ("a step cap that sets a flag") |


### 6.160 Stand-ins of the lumpy mesh of a small body (MAP.279)

`html/static/lumpymesh.js` draws a moon or an asteroid that its own weight has not pulled round (the roundness ratio under 1 that GEN.1158 and GEN.1159 store, carried on the body's look record) as an elongated, bumpy lump instead of a sphere. The shape is display-only noise from the look's seed. The numbers only shape a picture and feed nothing the generator stores.

| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| LUM1 | D | T9 | L0 | lumpymesh.LUMPY_MAX_KM 400: a body whose roundness is not stored is drawn as a lump under this radius | 300 to 600 km | a system stored before GEN.1159 | none (a drawing choice; the stored ratio replaces it for every system made after) | the stored roundness ratio of every body | Boss, 10:26 PM EDT 2026-10-23: lumpy meshes under about 400 km |
| LUM2 | P | T9 | L4 | lumpymesh.FALLBACK_ROUNDNESS 0.5: the ratio a lump without a stored one is drawn with | 0.2 to 0.8 | a lump of a system stored before GEN.1159 | none: a fitted picture | the stored roundness ratio | a choice of the item; no source |
| LUM3 | P | T6 | L0 | lumpymesh.MAX_ELONGATION 0.8: the long axis over the short is 1.8 at most for a body far from round (the longest small bodies of the Solar System are about two to one) | 0.5 to 1.2 | any lumpy body | a tri-axial shape stored for the body that differs by more than a factor 1.5 | a stored tri-axial shape | an inference from the small-body shapes of the Solar System |
| LUM4 | P | T9 | L3 | lumpymesh.BUMP_BASE 0.05 and BUMP_PER_DEFICIT 0.2: the bumps stand 5 percent of the radius high for a body just short of round, and 25 percent for one far from it | base 0.02 to 0.1; per deficit 0.1 to 0.3 | any lumpy body | none: tuned by eye against pictures of small bodies | a stored relief or a fitted power spectrum of small-body topography | tuned by eye against a sweep of pictures |
| LUM5 | P | T9 | L0 | lumpymesh.BUMP_WAVES 7: seven smooth waves, weighted 1/n, make the bumps | 4 to 12 | any lumpy body | none: a drawing choice | a measured spectrum of small-body shapes | a choice of the item |

### 6.161 Stand-ins of the mass of an asteroid field (GEN.1161)

`physics/field_mass.py` gives an interstellar asteroid field its mass by the belts' own rule (what the disc stretch it came from has left after the giants and the collisions, or the unbound share of two colliding rogue planets) and its stored bodies by the belt ledger. The two numbers below are the module's own; the rest is read from `belt_ledger`, `belt_mass`, `field_dispersal` and `rogue_collision`.

| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FM1 | P | T9 | L3 | field_mass.MIN_FIELD_MASS_EARTH 1e-9: a field under this mass is not a field (the belts' line, BG14) | 1e-10 to 1e-8 Earth masses | any field | a survey of warm dust around remnants that finds fields below the line, or none above it | the same replacement as BG14 | Boss, 2026-10-31: the belt minimum of 1e-9 Earth masses stands, the same rule for fields |
| FM2 | P | T9 | L4 | field_mass.COLLISION_LAW: the rubble of a rogue collision follows the rocky main-belt size law (`belt_ledger.ROCKY_LAW`) | the law's breaks and exponents | the stored bodies of a collision field | a measured or modelled largest-fragment mass of a planetary collision that differs from the law's by more than a factor 3 | the largest-fragment reading (LS12) | a stand-in with no source for the rubble of a planet-planet collision |

### 6.162 Stand-ins of the temperature a body took its shape at (GEN.1201)

`physics/shape_temperature.py` gives the roundness engine the temperature a body's weight was shaping it at: the surface temperature plus the accretion heat plus the heat its rock and the tides make in the time heat takes to leave it, capped at the rock's liquidus. The heat capacity of rock and metal and the diffusivity are `interior_heat`'s (Thiriet et al. 2019 as used by Guimond et al. 2022); the two numbers below are the module's own.

| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SHT1 | D | T7 | L0 | shape_temperature.ICE_SPECIFIC_HEAT 1700 J/(kg K): the heat capacity of the ice of a body (ice Ih is about 1500 at 160 K and 2100 at 273 K) | 1500 to 2100 | icy bodies between 150 and 273 K | a measured heat capacity of the ice of a body outside 1500 to 2100 | a temperature-dependent ice heat capacity read from a table | recalled from the known range of ice Ih; not read |
| SHT2 | P | T6 | L1 | shape_temperature.WINDOW_SHAPE_FACTOR pi^2: the heat a body keeps is the heat its rock makes in `R^2 / (pi^2 kappa)`, the decay time of the first conduction mode of a sphere; the heat is spread over the whole body, so the temperature is the centre's, an upper bound for the mean | factor 3 to 20 | any solid body | a thermal-history run (`interior_heat`) of a 10 to 100 km body whose peak mean temperature differs from this by more than 300 K | the peak temperature of the thermal history of `interior_heat`, once it follows planetesimals | a model choice of the item: the steady-state balance `h R^2 / (6 kappa)` gives 1.6 times the heat of this window, so the window is the smaller |
